Strong business SRCA and BTE; management; financial profile

F3967th of 79

Memo topic 3, Value chain, structure, model, financials, management: the Rater's score

4a

Business quality

D4752nd of 758 answers
Judgement47C-4150C-4236C-4348C-4458C-4557C-4630C-4745
Group judgement

Company - Competitive advantage

D4752nd of 75

Layer 1 · Company - Competitive advantage

Written last by the Investment Analyst for its group

GN has one genuine, defensible franchise: certified premium enterprise headsets. It is protected by certification, device management, channel embedding and audio know-how. It is gaining share against a stumbling number two, and it has applied pricing power. That franchise is about 60-65% of RemainCo.

The rest of the business has weaker advantages:

  • Video is subscale.
  • Gaming's moat is brand-led and confined to headsets.
  • Overall scale is too small to match Logitech's overhead or bundling breadth.

The advantages also do not show up in returns: return on invested capital is about 5-6% against Logitech's about 25%. On Towson's test, GN's advantage over rivals is visible in share and invisible in economics, so it is a franchise held back by its cost structure. That matters because cost structure is more fixable (C-77) than a weak franchise would be.

For the Consumer:

  • Model stable premium share and pricing.
  • Do not credit GN with structural gains in video or gaming.
  • Treat software substitution and bundling as slow, compounding risks to the 2028-2030 terminal assumptions.
C-41

Which sustainable relative competitive advantages or moats does the company hold (customer-related; production-related; scale-based; regulatory or political; culture or knowledge), how strong is each, and are they widening or narrowing?

F5058th of 75

Layer 1 · Company - Competitive advantage

Judgement: GN holds real but moderate customer-related advantages in enterprise headsets. The strongest is the switching cost created by certification and device management. They act more as barriers to new entrants than as advantages over the incumbent rivals, which hold the same certifications. They are widening slightly at the premium end and narrowing at the entry level. SteelSeries' advantages are weaker: brand and software in premium headsets only. There is no production, regulatory or scale moat of note outside the category.

Customer-related advantages.

Advantage Mechanism Strength and limits
Platform certification Teams, Zoom and Google Workspace certification is mandatory and takes years of joint engineering A strong barrier to entrants, but not exclusive: Poly, Logitech, EPOS and Yealink are certified. A barrier, not an advantage over rivals (the Wisdom Library's distinction)
Device-management software (Jabra+) IT administrators manage fleets remotely; secure models such as Engage 55 block trade-down Moderate switching cost
Installed base and brand Used by more than 80% of Fortune 100 companies; 92% of IT buyers decide on headsets and room video together Moderate; supports cross-selling
Channel embedding 20,000 resellers; funded specialists inside CDW Moderate; reproducible by a determined rival with money

Technology and knowledge. Audio signal processing and low-power on-device AI (the ClearVoice neural network in Evolve3). Moderate, and widening if Evolve3's premium lead holds. Exposed to software parity.

Production and scale. Manufacturing is outsourced. Purchasing scale (more than 20 million units, consolidated suppliers) gives modest cost advantages. Dual-sourcing is resilience, not a moat.

Regulatory. None in Enterprise. Military certification creates barriers around FalCom.

SteelSeries. The number-one brand in premium gaming headsets, GG software with 3-4 million active users, and direct engagement with users. Weak to moderate. It does not extend to mice and keyboards.

Direction, using the Towson test of advantage relative to rivals. Share against Poly has risen, a sign of a real advantage. Relative returns against Logitech are far lower (return on invested capital about 5-6% against about 25%), a sign the advantage does not convert into economics. Hard advantages are moderate; soft ones (execution, culture, alignment) are weak (C-59).

C-42

How large is the company's scale relative to competitors and other actors (revenue, headcount and so on), and what does that scale confer?

F3664th of 75

Layer 1 · Company - Competitive advantage

Judgement: RemainCo is large within its category and small against the companies that matter strategically. Revenue is about DKK 9.5bn (roughly USD 1.4-1.5bn), against Logitech at USD 4.3bn and HP's Personal Systems business at about USD 14.4bn a quarter. That scale gives category leadership in enterprise headsets and leaves GN subscale on overhead, R&D productivity, purchasing and bundling breadth.

  • What the scale gives.
  • The largest enterprise headset business, at about DKK 6bn of revenue.
  • R&D focused on audio.
  • Reach through more than 20,000 resellers and purchasing of more than 20 million units a year.
  • What it does not give.
  • Overhead efficiency: management and administration cost 9% of sales, against 3.5% at Logitech.
  • R&D productivity: R&D is about 9% of sales, against 6.5% at Logitech.
  • Purchasing power against Logitech in memory and microcontrollers.
  • A full workspace bundle like Logitech's or HP's.
  • Gaming scale: SteelSeries is about 6-14% of core gaming gear, against 20-22% for Logitech G and Razer.
  • What it means for the model. The margin gap to Logitech (EBIT margin 7.6% against about 16%) is mainly a scale and overhead gap, not a gross-margin gap (C-53). That is why DNB argues RemainCo "is sub-scale and unlikely to organically close the margin gap" and why break-up speculation persists. Headcount for RemainCo alone is not given in the files.

C-44

How high is the company's surplus customer utility against competing or alternative offerings, and is it leading, or at least demonstrably not inferior? (Hard to prove, easier to disprove; the aim is to disprove inferiority at Early-Stage and Deep Dive, for example Sophos, Bytes.)

D5844th of 75

Layer 1 · Company - Competitive advantage

Judgement: in premium enterprise headsets, GN's surplus value to customers is leading, or at least demonstrably not inferior. That is supported by evidence of share gain, sell-out and expert testimony. In entry-level headsets, video and lower-tier gaming, the offer is not leading and in places is inferior on price or scale.

  • Not inferior, and probably leading, at the premium end.
  • Evolve3 75/85 premium revenue grew over 50% and premium sell-out grew at double digits.
  • Experts credit Jabra with leading in design and marketing (former Poly engineering vice president) and with the best understanding of IT distribution (CDW).
  • Users praise Evolve2's folding design, hybrid noise cancellation and connectivity.
  • Evolve3 drops the boom arm for a consumer-style design and uses neural-network voice isolation.
  • Inferior or unproven.
  • Entry level: Yealink and Chinese brands are cheaper and closing the feature gap.
  • Video: Logitech holds over 50% of video bars and Cisco leads large rooms.
  • Gaming mice: an ageing portfolio.
  • Consumer-facing products (Jabra Enhance): weak traction.
C-45

To what extent does the company have, and use, demonstrable pricing power with minimal erosion of its relative surplus customer utility, shown by evidence of application rather than conjecture?

C+5721st of 75

Layer 1 · Company - Competitive advantage

Judgement: GN has demonstrated pricing power with little erosion of customer value. It applied roughly 10% US increases across Enterprise and Gaming in 2025 with "volume impact well below price gains". That is evidence of power that was applied, not conjecture. But it was used to pass on costs, not to raise prices in real terms, and it is materially weaker in Gaming and in European entry-level headsets.

  • Applied.
  • The ~10% US increases (Jefferies; DNB; CFO and CEO statements).
  • Enterprise underlying gross margin +110bp to 57.2% in Q2 2026.
  • The CEO stated that recent increases had negligible negative volume impact in Enterprise.
  • Gaming retailers received advance notice and passed increases quickly to consumers.
  • Logitech raising US prices by up to 25% at the same time corroborates industry-wide pass-through.
  • Limits.
  • Management concedes pricing power is "structurally limited in Gaming" because of consumer price elasticity.
  • Gaming retailers are reluctant to support increases.
  • Distributors seeking marketing funds cause modest average-price erosion.
  • European entry level faces low-cost competition.
  • The bear-case reports flag the risk that price increases mask volume declines, and a sign of this would be positive pricing with declining units.
  • The latent real gain is described in C-17.
C-46

To what extent does the company share its scale economies with customers, stated or de facto, in its messaging, strategy, culture or values?

F3054th of 75

Layer 1 · Company - Competitive advantage

Judgement: there is no evidence that GN shares scale economies with customers, either stated or in practice. It prices at a premium and keeps the margin. Its relative scale is too small for this to be a lever.

  • Nothing in messaging, strategy or culture describes passing cost savings to customers in the manner of Costco, Amazon or Ryanair.
  • Its 2025 behaviour was to pass costs on, not to share savings.
  • The obvious candidates for a model of sharing scale economies are Logitech (broad, efficient) or low-cost Chinese entrants (price-led).
  • GN's position is premium specialist, the opposite of that model.
C-47

To what extent do gravitational or mechanical forces, rather than heroic company dynamism, improve the company's competitive advantage and its financial outputs against peers with each period that passes?

D4551st of 75

Layer 1 · Company - Competitive advantage

Judgement: some forces that work mechanically, without heroic effort, favour GN: replacement of the installed base, recertification on each platform update, device management accumulating on Jabra+, and per-share effects from buybacks. They are about matched by mechanical forces that favour larger rivals: bundling gravity, software substitution and scale-driven overhead advantages. Improvement in GN's competitive position relative to peers therefore still depends on its own dynamism, such as a product cadence every 2-3 years.

  • Mechanical forces favouring GN.
  • The 2020-21 installed base reaching end-of-life, which creates demand without marketing.
  • Teams and Zoom recertification cycles, which favour incumbent certified vendors.
  • Jabra+ fleets, which make each further deployment stickier.
  • Contact-centre replacement every 18-20 months.
  • The financial mechanics: lower interest, lower share count, reduced amortisation after impairments.
  • Mechanical forces against GN.
  • Larger rivals' procurement bundles, which favour broad portfolios over time (the Wisdom Library notes 1-2 points of share a year in the medtech analogue).
  • Software improving at the operating-system level every year.
  • Logitech's overhead and purchasing scale.

This management team is good at big structural decisions and poor at delivering operating plans. The CEO's actions (One GN synergies, exiting consumer earbuds, relocating the supply chain, selling Hearing at a premium) show agency and a sound instinct for capital in the decisions that matter most to value per share. For the Consumer:

C-55

How big is the company's ambition or vision (a force: growth as a priority, outcompeting, innovating, adapting, pushing), particularly in relation to peers?

F3865th of 75

Layer 1 · Company - Management

Judgement: the ambition is moderate and now framed around crystallising value and returning capital rather than outcompeting rivals. Ambition for RemainCo's operations is lower than before and below its peers'.

  • The reset. The 2024 CMD ambition for the group (5-8% organic growth, 16-17% EBITA margin) is suspended. DNB expects RemainCo's new targets to be about 3-6% growth and about 12% margin.

C-56

How great and smart is the strategy, with right and bold strategic priorities (an enabler): widening the moat over the mid term, growth as a priority, and a dynamic strategy that can capitalise on a plausible range of environments?

F5063rd of 75

Layer 1 · Company - Management

Judgement: the strategy contains one smart, bold decision, the Hearing sale, and one sound defensive frame for RemainCo (premium specialist, cost reset, flexible supply, no M&A). It does not yet show a route to widening the moat, to growth as a priority, or to answering the bundlers. It is adaptable to a plausible range of environments mainly because of its balance sheet and its supply-chain agility.

  • The Hearing sale was right and bold. GN Hearing had lost share for years, from 21% in 2017 to about 10% in 2025, and faced vertically integrating rivals. Selling it for DKK 17.0bn, above the group's pre-deal market value of DKK 13.1bn, captured a premium from a strategic buyer.
  • The RemainCo frame. Premium certified audio, video cross-selling, product cadence, cost reset and dual-sourcing are coherent but defensive.
  • Gaps.
  • Scale against Logitech and HP.
  • Video share of only 3-4%.
  • Whether Gaming fits with Enterprise. The Wisdom Library's "linked businesses are not a conglomerate" test applies: Enterprise and Gaming share supply chain and procurement but not customers or channels. That points to separate valuation and a credible case for an eventual break-up.
  • Dynamism. Contract manufacturers can move a line in 8-12 weeks, there is net cash, and fixed assets are light. All help adaptation.

C-63

Explain, with the evidence, the extent to which the company puts shareholders first, ahead of management, and how prudent its governance is (scaffolding put in place even where it was not needed): what does the evidence show, and why?

C+5522nd of 75

Layer 1 · Company - Management

Judgement: the evidence shows governance that is prudent and improving, with real scaffolding in place. "Shareholders first" behaviour is now credible, but it was largely forced by past failures and shareholder pushback rather than being GN's long-standing nature.

  • For.
  • All six shareholder-elected directors are independent. There is a single share class with equal voting and no controlling owner.
  • Audit opinions from PwC have been unqualified since 2019.
  • Controllers visit subsidiaries and the audit committee reviews the whistleblower line quarterly.
  • Malus and clawback apply to cash bonuses and long-term incentives. Executives must hold shares equal to 100% of base salary within five years. Long-term incentives are capped at four times salary.
  • Related-party dealings are limited.
  • Management responded quickly to the 2015 Beltone fraud.
  • Today there is a large buyback, the dividend is reinstated and large M&A is ruled out.
  • Against.
  • Insider ownership is under 0.15%.
  • The DKK 8bn SteelSeries deal was done near the peak, on debt.
  • A rights issue of up to DKK 7bn was proposed in 2023 and withdrawn after large shareholders refused to support it.
  • Proxy advisers recommended voting against the 2022 remuneration report.
  • Aggressive capitalisation of R&D.
  • No shareholder vote on the Hearing sale (none was required).
  • Why it is improving now. Shareholders effectively vetoed further dilution in 2023, and the board has since put returns ahead of expansion.

4c

Financial profile

F3069th of 758 answers
Judgement30C-4950C-5040C-5122C-5240C-5345C-5422C-7678
Group judgement

Company - Economic strength

F3069th of 75

Layer 1 · Company - Economic strength

Written last by the Investment Analyst for its group

RemainCo's economics are currently weak and its earnings quality poor. Return on invested capital is about 5-6%, falling to 1.6-2.9% for 2026-27 on Bernstein's numbers. EPS is about zero in 2026. Adjusted EBITA is flattered by refunds and capitalisation. Working capital is about a third of revenue, and 2026 free cash flow is negative year to date.

The diagnosis, though, is specific and improvable. Gross margins are at or above Logitech's, so the gap lies in overhead and scale. High operating leverage, cuttable administration and heavy working capital mean that a modest volume recovery plus cost action can lift EBITA towards about 10-11% in 2027 and about 11-13% by 2028-29.

The per-share economics then strengthen mechanically: interest disappears, the share count falls by 20% or more, and normal cash tax replaces the old shield. For the Consumer, the key modelling choices are:

  1. The volume assumption, the dominant swing.
  2. The underlying 2026 base. Strip out the refund and normalise G&A.
  3. Capitalised development against amortisation, after the impairments.
  4. Working capital. Hold it at 30-35% of revenue unless there is evidence of release.
C-49

What earnings-per-share growth is the company generating, with what revenue growth, and how durable is it?

C-5032nd of 75

Layer 1 · Company - Economic strength

Judgement: consensus has EPS rising from about zero in 2026 (median DKK 0.05) to DKK 3.90 in 2027, DKK 5.68 in 2028 and DKK 8.65 in 2029 (the last year rests on one broker), on revenue growth of only about 4-5% a year. The EPS growth is therefore mostly recovery from trough margins, removal of interest cost and a 20%+ fall in the share count. Its durability is medium for the mechanical parts and low-to-medium for the operating parts.

  • The bridge to 2027.
  • Consensus EBIT rises from DKK 145m (2026, after one-offs) to DKK 699m (2027). That reflects the end of carve-out charges, DKK 200m of savings, volume leverage and impairment-reduced amortisation.
  • Net interest falls from about DKK 645m (2025, group) to near zero.
  • Average shares fall from 145.6m towards about 125m (my rough estimate, if DKK 1bn is bought back by March 2027 and DKK 3bn over the rest of the year).
  • Tax is about 22-23%.
  • History for context. Group EPS (brokers' basis) was DKK 6.82 (2023), 6.78 (2024) and 4.39 (2025) on a much larger business carrying leverage.
  • Durability.
  • Interest and share-count gains are durable once executed.
  • Savings are durable if not reinvested.
  • Operating margin recovery depends on demand.
  • The Wisdom Library's "leverage-to-EPS transmission" warns that consensus EPS CAGRs after deleveraging flatter the true quality of earnings. The Consumer should report per-share growth in operating EBITA separately from EPS.
C-50

How much immediate scope is there to deploy capital or operating spend at a high return on new invested capital for three to five years, and what does that mean for mid- and long-term potential and the fair multiple?

F4056th of 75

Layer 1 · Company - Economic strength

Judgement: there is little scope to deploy new capital at a high return over three to five years, beyond re-filling existing capacity. Incremental returns on recovering volume are very high because the fixed costs are already in place, but the business lacks a long runway for reinvestment. M&A is ruled out and new markets are small. Capital is therefore best returned. That caps the fair multiple at a cyclical-hardware level rather than a compounder level.

  • High incremental returns on recovery. Drop-through of about 40-60% (C-75), against working capital needs of about 30-35% of incremental revenue, gives incremental pre-tax returns well above 100% on recovered volume. This is a mechanical gain, not a reinvestment opportunity.
  • Opportunities that need new capital.
  • Video: competitive, subscale.
  • FalCom: small, lumpy.
  • AI on-device processing: R&D-intensive, at about 9% of sales.

  • SteelSeries categories. None offers large, high-return deployment.

  • The buyback. At an implied RemainCo enterprise value of about DKK 7-10bn, the buyback "earns" roughly the business's earnings yield: about 7-10% pre-tax on 2027 EBITA. That is acceptable, not outstanding.

  • Implication for the fair multiple. With low reinvestment, value comes from cash yield plus cyclical recovery. That supports roughly 8-11x EBITA for RemainCo (my judgement), not Logitech-plus multiples.
C-51

How high are the returns on capital: post-tax return on new invested capital, on capital employed and on tangible capital?

F2270th of 75

Layer 1 · Company - Economic strength

Judgement: returns on capital are low: group return on invested capital after tax was 5.6% in 2025, against 18.0% in 2019. Bernstein forecasts RemainCo's at only 1.6% in 2026 and 2.9% in 2027. The low figures reflect DKK 8bn of SteelSeries goodwill and intangibles and heavy working capital. Returns on tangible capital are materially higher and are the better guide to the economics of incremental volume.

  • Group returns. Pre-tax adjusted return on capital employed was 5.3% in 2025 against 19.5% in 2019. Return on invested capital was 5.6% against 18.0% (after tax).
  • Peers. Logitech's return on invested capital is about 25% and its return on equity 32.8%. The gap is more than 700bp (business-foundations digest).
  • Tangible capital, on my rough inference. RemainCo's tangible operating capital is dominated by working capital (DKK 3.35bn at Q3 2026), capitalised development (not separately given; I assume about DKK 1.5-2.5bn after impairments) and small fixed assets. On a 2027 EBITA of about DKK 1.0bn (about DKK 0.78bn after tax), return on tangible capital would be roughly 13-16%.
  • Return on new invested capital. Unknown historically for RemainCo. On recovering volume it is high (C-50).

C-53

How high are gross and operating profit margins, and how are they trending?

D4543rd of 75

Layer 1 · Company - Economic strength

Judgement: GN's gross margins are high and rising, at or above its main peer. Its operating margins are low and recovering from a trough. That split is the single most important diagnostic in the case: the gap to Logitech lies in operating costs (scale and overhead), not in price or product cost, which makes it more addressable by cost action.

  • Where the gap sits. Administration at 9% of sales against 3.5%, and R&D at 9% against 6.5%. That alone is about 8 points of the roughly 8-9 point gap.

C-54

How clean is the earnings base: how large is the gap between adjusted and reported earnings; do one-offs, capitalised costs or other items flatter it; and does the tax rate or any other non-obvious leakage erode earnings per share?

  • Group non-cash impairments of DKK 1.3bn in 2026.
  • DKK 750m of carve-out cash costs in 2026-27, outside adjusted figures.
  • Q2 2026 net income was DKK 119m below consensus because of the LXE Hearing financial impairment.
  • Items that flatter adjusted figures.
  • The DKK 153m tariff refund inside adjusted EBITA in Q3 2026. It is unusual for a one-off to be included in "adjusted".
  • The Q3 G&A correction of over-allocated carve-out costs.
  • Gaming provision releases (Q3 2024 and Q2 2026).
  • Capitalisation.
  • Capitalised development was DKK 1,034m in 2025 (DKK 1,015m in 2024) against DKK 1,460m expensed including amortisation. Logitech expenses all R&D Sonova capitalises only tooling and implants.
  • There was a DKK 63m write-down of development projects in Q4 2025, and R&D amortisation was restated in 2025 (overstated by about DKK 20m a quarter).
  • Other. Amortisation of acquired intangibles from the SteelSeries purchase sits outside EBITA. The amount is not in the files and must be found (T-P4).
  • Tax. The rate is about 22-23% (22.4% in 2025). The upfront tax on the sale fell to DKK 300-500m and the future tax asset shrinks with it.

For the model: build 2027 from an underlying 2026 base that excludes the refund and uses the nine-month average G&A. Model capitalised development and amortisation explicitly. Keep about DKK 190m of carve-out cash costs in 2027.

C-76

How material is the financial leverage or gearing: how much does it amplify earnings per share and risk, and how comfortable is it?

A787th of 75

Layer 1 · Company - Economic strength

Judgement: financial leverage swings from a high burden today (3.9x net debt to EBITDA, interest of about DKK 645m a year at group level) to net cash after closing. It will then be used deliberately: the gearing target is 1.0-1.5x net debt to EBITDA, reached by returning more cash than the company holds. At about 1.5x on about DKK 1.2bn of EBITDA, roughly DKK 1.8bn of net debt, that is comfortable. It amplifies EPS modestly and adds risk only if GN re-levers into a downturn, which is its historical failure mode.

  • Today. Net interest-bearing debt was DKK 9,578m at Q3 2026, with FactSet total debt of DKK 10.5bn. Facilities were extended to 2028 and 2030 in 2025. Rising interest rates had made the SteelSeries debt "highly expensive".
  • After closing. Net cash. Most gross debt will presumably be repaid; this needs confirming. Net interest will be near zero in 2027 and then modest.
  • History. Leverage peaked at 5.7-7.1x in 2022, depending on the definition, and forced dividends to be suspended and equity to be raised in 2023.
  • Comfort. High after closing. Watch the order of events: buybacks financed with debt before the Amplifon shares are sold, at the moment Europe might weaken.

Pricing power

3 answers

C-45To what extent does the company have, and use, demonstrable pricing power with minimal erosion of its relative surplus customer utility, shown by evidence of application rather than conjecture?C+5721st of 75full answer under 4a Business quality
P-04

After assessing the pricing power in greater detail, how is price likely to contribute to revenue and margin over the next 18 months to four years?

C4827th of 75

Layer 2 · Financial forecasts

Judgement: price will defend margin, not expand it. The 2025 US price rise (about 10% on Enterprise and Gaming, about 30% of RemainCo revenue in North America, so about 3% of group revenue) has been lapped by Q3 2026. Its "latent real gain", the part of the tariff cost later refunded or no longer paid, is already visible in the 2026 underlying gross margin (about 51.7-51.9% against 49.6% in FY2025) and is therefore in the base, not incremental for 2027. From 2027 I expect like-for-like price of about +1% a year in Enterprise and about 0% in Gaming, about +0.7% for the group, plus about +0.5% from premium mix. That roughly offsets wage, memory and residual tariff costs, so price and mix contribute about +0.7-1.2 points to revenue growth and about zero to the margin.

1. The evidence on what price did in 2025-2026.

  • Applied and held: about 10% in the US in 2025, rolled out as contracts allowed, earlier in Enterprise than in Gaming (Q2 2025 earnings call). List prices were held through 2026 after the DKK 153m refund in Q3 2026 (DKK 78m Enterprise, DKK 75m Gaming; Bernstein). The refund was a retroactive cost recovery, not a trigger for price concessions.
  • Volume response: small in Enterprise ("well below the price gains"); larger in Gaming (organic growth -12% in Q4 2025), followed by recovery (+5% in Q2 and Q3 2026, driven by headset share). Logitech's 10% US rise produced only a mid-single-digit volume fall (Morgan Stanley).
  • Gross margin: Enterprise underlying 57.2% in Q2 2026 (+110bp); RemainCo underlying about 51.7-51.9% in Q2-Q3 2026. Gaming's 39.2% in Q2 2026 included provision releases.

2. What remains to be gained from price, and why it is small.

  • The refund was largely one-off. US tariffs of 20% (Vietnam), 19% (Malaysia) and 15% (Germany) still apply to the relocated production (Bernstein). The latent real gain is therefore limited to the gap between what GN priced for in 2025 and the lower rate it now pays. It shows up as a higher gross margin in 2026, and I keep that level in 2027-2030. It is not a further step up.
  • New-generation pricing is flat like for like. Evolve3 85 at EUR 569 and Evolve3 75 at EUR 399 match the prior generation. The gain comes from mix (premium revenue +50%, double-digit premium sell-out), not from list price.
  • Pressure is rising at the edges. Lenovo's ThinkPad 8550 at about USD 150 against Jabra Evolve3 75 at about USD 350; Dell's five certified business headsets (November 2026), manageable through the Dell console; Yealink's European push; HP, Dell and Lenovo bundling with deep headset discounts in price-ranked tenders (expert calls). There is no measured tender discount or Jabra win rate in the files.
  • Gaming has little pricing power. Management concedes elasticity; Komplett reports that memory-driven price rises hurt consumer demand in Norway; retail promotional volumes are up 20-25% (channel check, August 2026).

3. Contribution by year (my base case).

2026 2027 2028-2030
Group price, like for like about +1% (the 2025 rise annualising in H1) about +0.7% about +0.5-0.7% a year
Premium mix (Evolve3, SteelSeries premium headsets, FalCom) about +0.5% about +0.5% about +0.5% a year
Effect on the gross margin +2.1-2.3 points (with tariff relief) about flat (price and mix against memory, wages and dual-sourcing) about flat

4. Paths and skew. The cone for price is narrow upward and wider downward. Upside: further tariff relief on Southeast Asian hubs (structural, +50-100bp of gross margin), or further refunds (one-off). Downside: a give-back of 2-3% on European entry-level and mid-tier headsets in bundled tenders, about -0.5% of group revenue and about DKK -50m of EBITA a year, or a gaming promotional war after Logitech's supply problem eases.

Insight for sets three and four. The sector rule that "the market keeps paying a high multiple while volume grows, not only price" applies directly. RemainCo's 2026 gross-margin gain is price and tariff relief, not volume. Anyone extrapolating the 2026 gross-margin step into 2027 is double counting, and a price-led story would not be rewarded with a multiple.

Operating leverage

2 answers
C-7550P-0158
C-75

How material is the fixed-cost operating leverage: how much does a given change in revenue move operating profit, and how wide a range of outcomes does that create?

D5037th of 75

Layer 1 · Company - Economic strength

Judgement: fixed-cost operating leverage is high. About 40-60% of each change in revenue drops through to EBITA, because about DKK 1.9bn of shared costs (about 20% of revenue) and most divisional operating costs are fixed in the short term. Each 1% of revenue moves EBITA by about DKK 40-55m (about 0.4-0.5 points of margin). A ±5% revenue range around 2027 consensus produces EBITA of roughly DKK 0.75-1.25bn (8-12.5% margin).

Evidence from 2026 against 2025, year on year.

Quarter Revenue change EBITA change Drop-through
Q1 -DKK 187m (-8.2%) -DKK 123m (from 129 to 6) ~66%, inflated by inventory provisions
Q2 -DKK 131m (-5.7%) -DKK 53m (from about 163 to 110) ~40%
Q3 +DKK 56m (+2.5%) +DKK 66m underlying (from 194 to about 260) >100%, flattered by savings starting, the G&A correction and gross margin

Structure. Gross margin about 50-52%. Variable selling costs (marketing funds, freight) reduce the contribution to about 40-45%. The CF report calculates ±1 point of revenue growth as ±16.7% on its 2026 EPS base.

The range for 2027. Around consensus revenue of DKK 9,974m and an EBITA of about DKK 1.0bn:

  • -5% revenue gives about DKK 0.75-0.8bn (about 8%).
  • +5% gives about DKK 1.2-1.25bn (about 12%).

For the model: treat the volume assumption as the biggest single driver of 2027-28 EBITA. Leverage helps in the current upswing and is dangerous if Europe weakens.

P-01

After assessing the fixed-cost operating leverage in greater detail, how is it likely to shape the main financial line items over the next 18 months to four years, against the last four to six years?

C+5821st of 75

Layer 2 · Financial forecasts

Judgement: fixed-cost operating leverage will be the main source of margin progress after 2027, but it will be weaker on the way up than on the way down, and it will matter more per share each year because the share count is shrinking. In my base case it lifts the adjusted EBITA margin from 7.6% underlying in 2026 to about 11.5% by 2030, roughly 1.5 points from savings in 2027 and about 2.5 points from volume over 2027-2030. That is a recovery, not a return to the economics GN Audio once had, and it leaves RemainCo well below Logitech's 14-18% EBIT margin.

1. The structure of the leverage, refined.

  • The fixed block is about DKK 1.9bn of shared costs below divisional profit (FY2025: divisional profit DKK 2,621m against adjusted EBITA DKK 727m; AlphaSense operating-leverage report). Divisional operating costs are also mostly fixed in the short term.
  • The variable layer is cost of goods (gross margin about 51.5-52% underlying in 2026) plus channel marketing funds, freight and royalties. That leaves a contribution margin of about 40-45% on each extra krone of revenue.
  • In a recovery, the realised drop-through will be lower, about 30-40%, for three reasons. Management has promised to "accelerate the launch cadence into 2027", which costs marketing and R&D. Wage inflation of about 3% on an operating cost base of about DKK 4.2bn is about DKK 125m a year, which price alone will not fully cover (P-04). And memory and semiconductor inflation pushes on product cost.
  • In a downturn, drop-through is higher, about 50-65% (Q1 2026: about 66%; Morgan Stanley's history: an 8% organic decline cut group EBITA 44%), because costs are cut with a lag of two to four quarters and the DKK 750m carve-out shows how expensive cuts are.
  • This asymmetry is the core judgement. The first set's "40-60% drop-through" is right as a structural number but should not be applied symmetrically: upside volume is worth about DKK 30-40m of EBITA per point of revenue, downside about DKK 50-60m.

2. What it does to the line items against history.

FY2019-FY2025 (group, FactSet) RemainCo FY2025 RemainCo forecast FY2027 / FY2030
Gross margin 41.7% (2022) to 53.1% (2019) 49.6% about 52% / 52%
Adjusted EBIT or EBITA margin 17.2% (2019) to 6.3% (2022), 10.7% (2025) 7.6% 9.7% / 11.5%
Revenue growth +18.7% (2022) to -6.2% (2025) continuing three-year CAGR about -10% (including the consumer exit) +3.0% / +3.5% a year
  • The group's 2019-2025 history is not a usable guide to RemainCo's margin level, because it mixed a 17% margin Hearing business with Audio. It is a usable guide to volatility: group EBIT margin halved between 2021 and 2022 on a revenue decline of only a few points in Audio, plus the SteelSeries debt. RemainCo, now wholly cyclical, will be at least as volatile.
  • The gross margin is not where leverage lives: it has been stable at 55-57% in Enterprise through 14 quarters of volume decline (C-28). Leverage lives in the shared-cost ratio, which falls from about 20% of revenue (2025) to about 17.5% (2030) in my base case, because the costs grow at about 2% a year and revenue at about 3.5%.

3. The quantified sensitivity, which the Consumer should carry into the third set.

  • 2027: each 1% of revenue (about DKK 97m) moves adjusted EBITA by DKK 30-40m up or DKK 50-60m down; about 0.35-0.6 points of margin; DKK 0.18-0.36 of EPS (3-6% of my EPS of 5.90).
  • 2028: the same 1% moves EPS by DKK 0.23-0.45 (3-6% of 7.85), because the same profit is spread over 102m shares rather than 129m.
  • Insight: the buyback raises, not lowers, the per-share sensitivity to the operating cycle. By 2029-2030 GN will be a smaller, re-levered (about 1.3x net debt to EBITDA in my base case) equity claim on a cyclical business. The EPS line will look smoother only because the falling share count adds about 12-27% a year to EPS mechanically (12% in 2027, 27% in 2028, 22% in 2029, 16% in 2030); underneath, net income sensitivity to revenue is unchanged and per-share sensitivity rises by about 40% between 2026 and 2028.

4. The cone for 2027 adjusted EBITA.

Case Organic growth 2027 Revenue Adjusted EBITA Margin EPS (model basis)
Downside: European corporate IT freeze, consumer weaker -2% about 9,250 about 700 7.6% about 4.5
Base +3.5% 9,730 940 9.7% 5.90
Upside: management's case, Evolve3 cycle plus easy comparisons +6% about 9,970 (consensus) about 1,060 10.6% about 6.6

The cone is slightly skewed to the downside on EBITA, because drop-through is asymmetric and Europe (about half of revenue) has not turned. It is much narrower on EPS than on EBITA, because the interest and share-count effects are locked in.

Where I differ from the first set. C-75 put a ±5% revenue range around consensus at DKK 0.75-1.25bn of EBITA. I put the centre lower (DKK 940m, not about DKK 1.0bn), because consensus revenue is about 2.5% above my base, and the upside wing lower (about DKK 1.06bn at consensus revenue), because I deduct reinvestment and cost inflation from the raw contribution margin.

Financial leverage

P-02

After assessing the financial leverage in greater detail, how is it likely to shape earnings per share and the balance sheet over the next 18 months to four years?

A861st of 75

Layer 2 · Financial forecasts

Judgement: the financial-leverage swing is the most powerful and most predictable driver of EPS over the next 18 months, and it then reverses slowly. Net financial items move from about DKK -540m in 2026 to about DKK +50m in 2027, worth about DKK 3.1 of the DKK 4.2 rise in EPS (model basis) between 2026 and 2027. From 2028, the deliberate re-levering towards 1.0-1.5x net debt to EBITDA lowers net financial items back to about DKK -130m by 2030, which costs about 10% of EPS against an unlevered path but is more than repaid by the extra shares retired. The balance sheet moves from 3.9x (Q3 2026) to net cash (end-2026) and back to about 1.3x by 2030. That is comfortable unless re-levering coincides with a European downturn.

1. The 2026-2027 bridge, where financial leverage dominates.

  • EPS (model basis) rises from 1.74 (2026, including the DKK 153m refund) to 5.90 (2027). Holding the share count constant, the swing in net financial items (+DKK 590m, or DKK 454m after tax) is worth about DKK 3.1 a share. The rise in adjusted EBITA (+DKK 71m reported; +DKK 224m excluding the 2026 refund) is worth DKK 0.4-1.2. The lower share count is worth DKK 0.7.
  • Insight for the third set: the most predictable part of the 2027 EPS increase is the largest part. It depends on closing (all approvals met on 6 November 2026) and on repaying the gross debt, not on demand.

2. Why my 2027 net financial items are positive and Bernstein's are DKK -461m.

  • My +50 is: interest on average net cash of about DKK 1.4bn at about 2.3% (+30); a dividend on the 56m Amplifon shares of about EUR 0.29-0.30 a share, about DKK 120m, received in spring 2027 if the shares are held at the record date (+120); interest on RemainCo lease liabilities (-20); bank fees, hedging costs and negative carry on any gross debt that cannot be repaid at once (-80).
  • Bernstein's DKK -461m (2027) and DKK -475m (2028) is close to the group's pre-sale interest bill. It is consistent only with GN keeping its debt while holding cash, which would contradict the company's stated plan and DNB Carnegie's model (net financial cost about DKK -3m in 2027). I treat Bernstein's figure as an error or an extreme assumption, not a view to weight.
  • The real uncertainties are (a) whether some instruments carry prepayment costs or cannot be repaid before maturity (facilities run to 2028 and 2030, extended in 2025; the instrument mix is not in the files), and (b) how the Amplifon shares are accounted for: if at fair value through profit and loss, their price swings will hit reported net financial items and must be stripped out of adjusted EPS.

3. The re-levering path (my base case).

Year-end Net cash (+) / debt (-), DKKbn, excluding leases and the Amplifon stake Net debt to EBITDA What drives it
2026 +3.0 net cash Closing proceeds, debt repaid, DKK 0.15bn of buybacks in December
2027 +0.6 net cash DKK 3.85bn of buybacks, partly funded by about DKK 1.15bn of Amplifon shares sold after the first lock-up release (about September 2027)
2028 +0.3 net cash DKK 3.0bn of buybacks, funded by the remaining Amplifon shares (about DKK 2.3bn; lock-up ends about March 2028) plus free cash flow
2029 -1.4 about 0.8x DKK 2.0bn of buybacks, partly debt-funded
2030 -2.5 about 1.3x DKK 1.5bn of buybacks
  • Insight: the "at least DKK 4bn by end-2027" is fundable without any re-levering if about a third of the Amplifon stake is sold in late 2027. Even with no Amplifon sale in 2027, end-2027 net debt would be only about DKK 0.6bn (about 0.4x). The 1.0-1.5x gearing target is therefore not a 2027 constraint; it is a statement about the 2028-2030 programme, which implies a cumulative further DKK 5-7bn of returns after 2027. That second wave is not in consensus, which carries no 2028-2029 buyback in most visible models except DNB Carnegie's DKK 3bn in 2028.
  • Risk. GN's historical failure mode was leverage into a downturn (5.7-7.1x in 2022). At 1.3x on my 2030 EBITDA, a 25% EBITDA fall takes leverage to about 1.8x, uncomfortable but nowhere near 2022. The dangerous sequence would be borrowing to buy back in 2028-2029 just as Europe weakens, with the Amplifon proceeds already spent. Watch the order of events, not the level.

4. Effect on equity and book value. Equity falls sharply as buybacks exceed earnings: about DKK 10.5bn of returns over 2027-2030 against cumulative net income of about DKK 2.6bn. Consensus book value per share (DKK 75-89 a share) and equity (DKK 11.3-13.0bn) assume far smaller returns and should not be used. Return on equity will rise mechanically; it says nothing about business quality.

P-03

After assessing the scope for cost elimination and efficiency in greater detail, how much is likely to reach the main financial line items over the next 18 months to four years, and when?

C-5432nd of 75

Layer 2 · Financial forecasts

Judgement: about DKK 200m of cost elimination is certain to reach the cost base by the start of 2027, but only about DKK 120-130m of it reaches adjusted EBITA in 2027 after reinvestment and inflation, and none of it improves the business against its pre-sale structure, because it only offsets stranded costs. A further DKK 100-200m is plausible over 2028-2030 (administration still about 9% of sales against Logitech's 3.5%). I include only about DKK 100m of that in the base case, because the expiry of the transitional service agreements with Amplifon (up to 24 months after closing) will add standalone IT and administration costs at the same time. Research and development should not be cut, and the consensus fall in capital spending to 4.6% of revenue by 2029 should be rejected.

1. The DKK 200m programme: what, when and how much reaches profit.

  • Content: about 300 roles, mostly administration and finance; restructuring of the Warsaw finance service centre; announced 6-7 May 2026; full run-rate from early 2027 (company; AlphaSense operating-leverage report). Management sized it against DKK 200m of stranded costs; DNB Carnegie puts the stranded costs at about DKK 150m.
  • Timing of the profit effect in my base case: about DKK 30m in Q4 2026; about DKK 170m more in 2027 (fully in each 2027 quarter).
  • What reaches adjusted EBITA in 2027 after the offsets: about DKK 120-130m (about 1.3 points of margin). I deduct about DKK 40m of reinvestment in launches and channel support, which management has signalled. Ordinary wage inflation is netted against price in P-04 rather than against the savings.
  • Confidence: high. One GN delivered DKK 430m in 2024 and was on track for DKK 600m by end-2026; Demant raised its programme from DKK 250m to DKK 300m. GN's record on cost is the strongest part of its delivery record (P-06).
  • What the CFO's "around 2% in 2027" means, read carefully. It is 2 points of margin against a continuing base that already carries the stranded costs. It is a recovery of a self-inflicted loss, not a gain against GN Audio's pre-sale economics. The market should not capitalise it as new margin.

2. The cash cost and payback. DKK 750m of one-off cash costs over 2026-2027 (about 75% in 2026), of which only about 30% is allocated to continuing operations in the income statement; the rest is transaction and separation cost. Against DKK 200m a year, the restructuring part pays back in about two years; the whole DKK 750m in about four. In my inputs the cash costs are netted into the disposals line (about DKK 560m in 2026, about DKK 190m in 2027), not into free cash flow.

3. Further scope, and what blocks it.

Area Gross scope 2028-2030 In my base case Why not more
Administration and central functions (about 9% of sales) DKK 150-250m if overhead moves halfway to Logitech about DKK 100m net, phased 2028-2030 Subscale: Logitech has three times the revenue; the transitional service agreements expire and standalone IT has to be built or bought; TSA costs are temporarily inside the base and obscure the true standalone cost
R&D (incurred about DKK 830m a year, about 8.7% of sales, of which about 70% capitalised in Q3 2026) DKK 100-200m none It funds the product cadence on which premium share depends (C-41); Plantronics after Polycom shows what under-investment does to a specialist
Sales and marketing Flexible in a downturn none in the base; a downturn lever Channel funding defends share against bundlers
Product cost Limited none Dual-sourcing, memory inflation, loss of Hearing's purchasing scale
Working capital (cash, not profit) to 25% of revenue would release about DKK 0.6-0.7bn from about 32% at end-2026 to 30% by 2030, about DKK 0.2bn One distributor bought DKK 2,001m in 2025; its terms are not disclosed and cannot be forced

4. Two accounting levers that can move reported EBITA without any real saving, which the Consumer must watch.

  • Capitalisation of development cost. In Q3 2026 GN capitalised DKK 150m of DKK 207m incurred (about 72%), against a stated historical rate of about 53-57%. Development amortisation rose to DKK 100m in Q3 2026 from DKK 66m. If the CMD on 24 February 2027 moves to a lower capitalisation rate, as Logitech, Corsair and most peers effectively do, adjusted EBITA would fall by up to DKK 100-200m a year with no change in cash or economics. This is a reporting risk to the margin path, not a business risk.
  • The 2026 impairments. Here I disagree with the first set (C-20, C-54). It expected the DKK 1.3bn of impairments (DKK 375m of them in RemainCo, against R&D, IT and facilities) to lower future amortisation and "mechanically support adjusted EBITA from 2027". The evidence since then points the other way: development amortisation rose from DKK 66m to DKK 100m a quarter in Q3 2026 as Evolve3 went into amortisation, and Jefferies expects completing Evolve3 to add about 1 point of D&A. I therefore model D&A, excluding PPA amortisation, rising from about DKK 510m (2026) to DKK 560m (2027) and DKK 610m (2030), and give no "big bath" tailwind.

5. Reject the consensus capital-spending path. Consensus capital spending, including capitalised development, falls from DKK 1,015m (2026) to DKK 502m (2029). For RemainCo, continuing capitalised development alone runs at about DKK 600m a year, and fixed assets and software add about DKK 100-150m. A fall to DKK 500m would require either deep R&D cuts (endangering the product cadence) or a switch to expensing (cutting EBITA). Neither is in management's stated plan. I hold capital spending at DKK 720-750m a year. That alone lowers 2029 free cash flow by about DKK 230m against consensus.

Reinforcing feedback loops

P-05

After assessing in greater detail whether reinforcing or balancing feedback loops are at work in the business model, such that dynamics at play today create additional scope for further positive/negative dynamics in the near/mid-term (reinforcing feedback loops), or the opposite (balancing loops), how are they likely to shape the main financial line items over the next 18 months to four years?

A626th of 75

Layer 2 · Financial forecasts

Judgement: over 2027-2029 the strongest feedback loop is a financial, reinforcing one: buybacks funded by sale proceeds, interest removal and then re-levering, which compounds EPS whatever demand does. The operating loops are weaker and roughly balanced. The reinforcing installed-base refresh and operating leverage are offset by balancing channel, bundling and gaming-elasticity loops. Two of the financial loops carry self-limiting features the Consumer must model: buyback accretion falls as the share price rises, and the Amplifon share price sets the size of the 2028 wave.

1. The loops, their direction and their size on the line items.

Loop Type Mechanism Effect on my base case Strength 2027-2029
Capital return Reinforcing (EPS) Proceeds and later debt retire shares; fewer shares raise EPS and dividend capacity per share Average shares 145.6m (2026) to 84m (2029); about 75% of EPS growth from 2027 onwards Strongest; contracted for 2027; probable for 2028
Interest removal One-off step Debt repaid; interest cost disappears Net financial items -540 to +50 (2026 to 2027) Large; one step, not a loop
Re-levering Reinforcing for EPS, balancing for risk Borrowing to buy back raises EPS and the risk of a forced stop in a downturn Net debt about 1.3x by 2030; net financial items back to -130 Medium; the risk arrives in 2029-2030
Operating leverage Reinforcing in both directions Volume over a fixed shared-cost base +2.5 points of margin 2027-2030 in the base; asymmetric (P-01) Medium
Installed-base refresh and Jabra+ lock-in Weakly reinforcing The 2020-21 base reaches end-of-life; device management and recertification favour the incumbent Supports flat premium share; does not lift growth above the market's 3-5% Weak-medium; corporate IT is "sweating assets" (Morgan Stanley CIO survey, 16 November 2026)
Channel inventory Balancing After destocking, sell-in rises only to sell-out; high rates keep distributor stocks permanently lower No restocking boost; Q4 2026 to Q1 2027 risk from GN's own DKK 205m inventory build Medium in 2027, fades
Bundling by PC makers Balancing, slow Price-ranked tenders favour broad portfolios Base: about -0.3% a year of share at European entry and mid-tier, inside my 3.5% organic growth Slow; the 2028-2030 threat
Gaming price elasticity Balancing Price rises and memory inflation cut units Gaming growth held at about 4% a year Medium
Amplifon share price Balancing for distributions Audiologist backlash and integration risk depress Amplifon, the stake funds the 2028 wave 10% on Amplifon is about DKK 345m of proceeds Medium
Talent loss in the carve-out Balancing, unquantified Departures slow the product cadence Not in the base; a downside trigger Low-medium

2. The two self-limiting features of the capital-return loop (quantified).

  • Price reflexivity. If buybacks over 2027-2028 are done at prices 10% above my assumptions (DKK 120-130), about 4m fewer shares are retired by mid-2028 and 2028 EPS is about 4% lower. A successful re-rating therefore slows the per-share compounding that drives it. Conversely, a share-price fall raises accretion. That is a stabiliser for value per share, provided management keeps buying when the shares fall (the first set's test, C-64).
  • Amplifon dependence. A 20% lower Amplifon price at sale cuts proceeds by about DKK 0.7bn. If not replaced by debt, about 5m fewer shares are retired and 2029 EPS is about 6% lower. Amplifon fell 15% on its Q3 2026 results and trades at a 30% discount to hearing peers (Jefferies). This is the cross-company loop the first set flagged (C-09), and it is the largest identifiable sensitivity in 2028-2029 EPS after volume.

3. Which dominate. The reinforcing financial loops (buybacks, interest) clearly outweigh the balancing operating loops for EPS over 2027-2029, by roughly 3 to 1 in their contribution to EPS growth. For EBITA and free cash flow, the operating loops are roughly balanced. The installed-base refresh and operating leverage add about 1 point a year of growth and margin; channel, bundling and elasticity take away about half of that. The case therefore rests on reinforcing loops that do not depend on the business improving, and on operating loops that are, at best, mildly positive. That is why my EBITA path sits below management's and why the EPS path can still beat consensus.

Where I differ from the first set. The first set treated the buyback as an unambiguous positive (C-19, score 80). The loop analysis shows that its accretion depends on two prices GN does not control (its own and Amplifon's), and that it converts RemainCo into a progressively more levered equity. That does not change the direction; it widens the cone of per-share outcomes from 2029.

Most of that gap is a difference of definition that has not been reconciled, with some credibility discount layered on top.

  • FY2026.
  • Guidance: organic growth 0-3%, adjusted EBITA margin 9-10% (including DKK 100-150m of refunds; DKK 153m already booked).
  • Consensus revenue of DKK 9,510m is -0.6% reported, which implies roughly +1% organic after a currency drag of about 2 points. That is the low half of the range.
  • DNB expects "the high end of 9-10%". Consensus EBIT of DKK 145m (median; mean DKK 281m) sits after carve-out one-offs and impairments, so it cannot be compared with adjusted EBITA.
  • Either way the Q4 bar is high: about DKK 330-420m of EBITA, an 11-14% margin.
  • FY2027.
  • The CFO (7 May 2026) said savings would add "around 2%" to underlying margins in 2027, giving a 10-11% run-rate. DNB models 10.4%.
  • Consensus EBIT is DKK 699m (7.0%) and consensus EBITDA DKK 1,205m (12.1%).
  • The gap between EBITA and EBIT is the amortisation of acquired intangibles (SteelSeries purchase accounting) plus any remaining one-offs (about DKK 190m of carve-out cash costs fall in 2027). Neither amount is in the files.
  • If purchase-accounting amortisation is about DKK 250-300m (unverified), consensus and management roughly agree. If it is small, consensus sits about 3 points below management.
  • Why the two could differ. Brokers discount a management team that has missed initial guidance for five years. Management's informal benchmark assumes volume recovers.

D-02

To what extent does past and present fundamental delivery make the expected change plausible, and to what extent does the thesis rely only on strengths the business demonstrably has, rather than on strengths it would need to acquire?

D7037th of 75

Layer 3 · Risks

Judgement: the expected change is very plausible where it is financial, plausible where it is cost, and only half-supported where it is commercial. Measured by earnings per share, about nine tenths of the change over the next 18 months rests on things GN has already done or holds in hand: regulatory clearance, cash proceeds, executed cost actions. Measured by operating profit, about two thirds of the 2027 improvement rests on demonstrated strengths and one third on a return to growth that GN has shown in one quarter of the last fifteen. The thesis needs no strength GN lacks to deliver 2027. It needs one to deliver 2028-2030: volume growth at the market rate in Europe, against bundlers and low-cost certified rivals.

1. Each expected change, tested against delivery.

Expected change Size in my base case Has GN shown it can do this? Verdict
Interest cost removed Net financial items from -540 (2026) to +50 (2027): about DKK 3.1 of the DKK 3.9 rise in 2027 earnings per share after PPA amortisation It needs only closing and repayment. All approvals were met on 6 November 2026. The debt is prepayable bank debt Demonstrated

Gaming growth of 3-4.5% Small SteelSeries reached first place in gaming headsets across all price tiers in Q2 2026. It is third in mice and keyboards Demonstrated in headsets only

2. Strengths the thesis uses, and strengths it would need.

  • Uses, and GN has them: about 46% of enterprise headset revenue; Teams and Zoom certification; fleet-management software; an underlying gross margin of about 52%; cost and supply-chain execution; a balance sheet about to hold net cash.
  • Would need, and GN lacks them:
  • A price answer at the middle and entry level. Dell launched five certified business headsets in November 2026 at USD 60-140, managed from the same console as its PCs. Jabra's new mid-tier models list at USD 199-389. Yealink earns a 65% gross margin and can fund whatever price it chooses.
  • A bundle. HP, Dell and Lenovo sell the PC, the screen and the headset together. Jabra's share of meeting-room video is 3-4%.
  • Scale in overhead.
  • The line between the two lists is the line between 2027 and 2028.
  • In 2027 easy first-half comparisons, the product cycle and the cost programme are enough. None needs a new strength.
  • From the second half of 2027 growth must come from European mid-market buyers choosing Jabra over a cheaper or bundled alternative. They must do so in a year when PC shipments are forecast to fall about 11% (Jefferies, 25 November 2026) and chief information officers plan to raise peripheral spending by 2.0%, and by 0.1% at the largest companies (Morgan Stanley, October 2026).

3. What the precedents say.

  • No precedent matches GN's starting point. The research found no specialist hardware company that sold its most defensive division, kept the cyclical ones and then restored margins. The successes kept their core and sold the weak part (Demant with its headset business, Sonova with consumer audio), or had three times GN's scale and expensed all development cost (Logitech).
  • What separated success from failure, in order of weight: 1. A balance sheet without debt. Plantronics failed for lack of it. GN will have it. 2. Speed in removing stranded cost. Honeywell expects to do it within 12 months. Unilever took three years. Siemens Healthineers expects the costs to arrive years before the savings. 3. Product-led growth above the market (Logitech, Sonova).
  • GN's position against these tests. It passes the first. It passes the second better than most, because its cost actions were completed before closing, so the savings and the deconsolidation arrive together in Q1 2027. It has not passed the third.
  • One caution on the second test. The transitional service agreements end up to 24 months after closing, and precedents show a second wave of stranded cost at that point (SKF's current separation carries recurring dis-synergies of SEK 1.1-1.2bn). For GN that falls in late 2028: outside the 18 months, inside the model.

4. How much of the forecast survives if growth does not come. With no organic growth in 2027, EBITA would be about DKK 820-840m (the cost saving less higher amortisation) and earnings per share on the model basis about DKK 5.2-5.3, about 8% below my base. The thesis therefore depends little on undemonstrated strengths for 2027 earnings per share. It depends heavily on them for the 2028-2030 margin path and for any re-rating, which the sector precedents tie to volume growth (C-84).

D-04

How wide is the cone of plausible outcomes over the next 18 months, how skewed is it, and so how predictable is the case?

D3852nd of 75

Layer 3 · Risks

Judgement: the cone is narrow for direction and wide for level, and it is skewed to the downside. In every plausible case 2027 earnings per share are several times 2026's, because the removal of interest is common to all cases. Around its centre, though, the 2027 figure is as uncertain as operating profit, not less: about -28% to +18% on earnings per share after PPA amortisation, against about -23% to +15% on EBITA. Here I disagree with the second set, which called the earnings-per-share cone "much narrower" than the EBITA cone. The case is predictable enough to say that earnings will be transformed. It is not predictable enough to say by how much to within a fifth.

1. The cone for 2027.

Downside Base Upside
What happens European corporate IT spending freezes; the consumer weakens As in my inputs Europe turns; mid-tier Evolve3 sells through; management's case
Organic growth about -2% about +3% about +6%
Adjusted EBITA (margin) about 700 (7.6%) 912 (9.4%) about 1,050 (10.5%)
Earnings per share, model basis about 4.5 5.73 about 6.5
Earnings per share after PPA amortisation about 3.2 4.42 about 5.2
Probability (my judgement) about 25% about 50% about 25%
  • Why the earnings-per-share cone is not narrower than the EBITA cone.
  • Once interest has gone, nothing below operating profit cushions a change in it.
  • PPA amortisation of about DKK 220m is a fixed deduction, so a 23% fall in EBITA is a 28% fall in earnings after it.
  • What is locked in is the floor of the cone, not its width. Even the downside figure of about DKK 3.2 is more than six times 2026's DKK 0.49.
  • A small stabiliser. In the downside the share price would be lower and the same DKK 4bn would retire more shares. In the upside it would retire fewer. This narrows the cone by one to two points in 2027 and by more in 2028 (P-05).
  • For 2028 the cone on earnings per share after PPA amortisation is about DKK 4.3 to DKK 7.3 around DKK 6.16: -30% to +18%. It is wider because the Amplifon proceeds enter. Each 10% on Amplifon's share price is about DKK 0.4-0.5bn, or about 3.5m GN shares.
  • A rule of thumb for the Consumer. Each point of 2027 revenue moves EBITA by about 4% when rising and about 6% when falling, and earnings per share after PPA amortisation by about 5% and 8%.

2. Why the cone is skewed to the downside.

  • Drop-through is asymmetric. A point of revenue is worth DKK 30-40m of EBITA on the way up and DKK 50-60m on the way down (P-01).
  • The centre of market expectations is above mine. Consensus revenue for 2027 is 3% above my base, and consensus EBITA about 5-9% above.
  • The evidence that arrived this week leans one way.
  • HP guided its 2027 earnings below consensus on 25 November 2026, with memory and storage now about 40% of the cost of a PC.
  • Jefferies expects commercial PC shipments to fall 8% in 2027. Dell reports customers deferring PC refresh.
  • Logitech's sales out of European channels fell 7% in its September quarter.
  • Distributors' inventories are high (TD Synnex at 70 days against 58; Bechtle up 75%), although none discloses how much of it is Jabra's.
  • The first quarter of 2027 is exposed.
  • GN built DKK 205m of inventory in Q3 2026 for a fourth quarter that must grow about 5%. Mid-tier Evolve3 sales so far are sales into the channel.
  • If Q4 2026 is met partly by filling distributors who then cut stock at their year-end, Q1 2027 pays for it.
  • This is why I have cut Q1 2027 growth from +5.5% to +3.5% and the year from +3.0% to +2.5%, and lowered the Q1 margin.
  • The record. Initial growth expectations were missed in each of the last five years.
  • Against these. First-half 2027 comparisons are very easy (Enterprise fell 5% and 7% in the first two quarters of 2026). North America and Asia-Pacific are growing. FalCom's deferred projects fall into 2027. Memory prices may ease in the second half of 2027 (spot prices for flash memory have fallen 25% since September).

3. The cone on the items that are not operating.

Item Range What decides it
Capital returned by end-2027 DKK 4bn or more, with about 85-90% probability Closing in December; authorisation on 10 March 2027
Shares at end-2027 about 111-116m The price paid (DKK 105-140)
Net financial items, 2027 about -70 to +80 Whether Amplifon pays its dividend in May 2027; how the stake is accounted for
Amplifon proceeds, 2027-2028 about DKK 3.0-5.0bn Amplifon's share price about nine and 15 months after closing
Reported, as opposed to adjusted, earnings Wide The accounting for the Amplifon stake; about DKK 190m of carve-out costs; a write-off of loan fees

4. What could move the cone suddenly, and how large such events have been.

  • The candidates are a shock at the largest distributor, a tariff change on Vietnam or Malaysia, and a component shortage.
  • Logitech's supplier failure in June 2026 was first sized at USD 200m of lost revenue in a quarter and ended at USD 70-80m after mitigation. Shocks of this kind are real, and tend to shrink by half or more within a quarter.
  • The source reports disagree on the tariff now applied to Vietnam (20% in one; a 12.5% measure from July 2026 in another). The size of that risk is therefore itself unclear.

5. What this means for predictability. Three statements can be made with high confidence: the interest bill goes, about DKK 4bn is returned by end-2027, and the DKK 200m is saved. One cannot: where revenue lands within a range of about eight points. Because of the fixed-cost base, that one unknown sets most of the width of the cone.

D-05

How helpful is the company's disclosure on operating momentum and key drivers of the thesis to monitor the case over about the next six months?

C+6021st of 75

Layer 3 · Risks

Judgement: disclosure is good enough to monitor the case closely over the next six months, and unusually good for the financial bridge, which can be followed week by week. It is weakest on the one thing that decides the operating cone, European end demand, where the company gives adjectives, not numbers. The six months to late May 2027 contain four scheduled company events and a change of reporting basis. The amount of new information will be high. The continuity of the series will be poor.

1. What the company discloses that serves the thesis.

  • The per-share bridge: excellent. Buybacks under the Safe Harbour rules are reported weekly, with shares bought and prices paid, so the share count and the average price can be tracked almost as they happen. The lock-up dates on the Amplifon shares are public, and Amplifon's price is quoted daily. Net debt is reported quarterly.
  • The cost bridge: good. Stranded costs, savings, carve-out cash costs, impairments and tariff refunds are each quantified. The refund is split by division (DKK 78m Enterprise, DKK 75m Gaming).
  • Development cost: good. Incurred, capitalised and amortised amounts are given every quarter. That is what allowed the second set's capitalisation figure to be corrected.
  • Growth and margin by division: adequate. Organic growth, gross margin and divisional profit are reported quarterly for Enterprise and Gaming, with revenue by region.

2. What is missing.

  • Sales out of the channel and channel inventory, in numbers. The company says "double-digit sell-in", "more equal balance" and "stable inventories". None of the five large distributors discloses its Jabra stock.
  • Growth by region within each division, and the split between price and volume. Europe is described as "challenging but improved".
  • A stable definition of underlying profit. Tariff refunds sit inside "adjusted" EBITA. The administrative-cost allocation was corrected in Q3 2026, and the CFO asked investors to use the nine-month average.
  • The stand-alone cost base. Fees under the transitional service agreements, and the cost base after they end, are undisclosed.
  • A defined adjusted earnings per share. None exists. That is why brokers' 2027 figures range from about DKK 1 to nearly DKK 5 (D-06).

3. The calendar: what will be learned, and when.

Date Event What it tells the Consumer
By end-December 2026 Closing; the buyback starts Debt repaid; opening net cash; the first weekly buyback data
Late January 2027 Logitech's December-quarter results European peripheral demand and holiday gaming, two weeks before GN reports
11 February 2027 FY2026 results, annual report, first FY2027 guidance Whether Q4 2026 reached about +5% organic growth and an 11-12% margin; Enterprise growth before the FalCom effect; the accounting for the Amplifon stake; leases; the terms of debt repayment; the guidance measure
24 February 2027 Capital Markets Day Mid-term targets and their definition; capital allocation after 2027; development-cost policy
10 March 2027 Annual meeting Authorisation of the DKK 3bn; the shares retired under the first DKK 1bn
29 April 2027 Q1 2027 report The first quarter with the savings and without the interest; whether Q4 sales into the channel sold through
May 2027 Amplifon's dividend About DKK 120m of 2027 financial income, or not

4. Two features that reduce the value of this disclosure.

  • The basis changes just as the perimeter does.
  • From 2027 guidance is expected to move from EBITA to EBITDA under IFRS 18 (DNB Carnegie), in the first year RemainCo reports alone.
  • The first clean year-on-year comparison on one definition will not exist until 2028.
  • On my reading, the company's EBITDA leaves amortisation of capitalised development inside the measure. The move therefore does not hide the capitalisation question, as one research report suggests it would. It does make every margin about 2 points higher than its EBITA equivalent, which invites misreading.
  • Guidance is tested late. FY2027 guidance is given on 11 February and first tested on 29 April, eleven weeks later. In 2026 the sequence was guidance in February, a cut in May and a second cut in August.

5. How to use it.

  • Within six months the Consumer will know from hard data whether the financial bridge has been delivered: closing, about 8-9m shares retired by March, the authorisation.
  • The Consumer will also have two readings on the operating bridge (Q4 2026 and Q1 2027) and management's own targets.
  • That is enough to move the base case within the cone of D-04. It is not enough to remove the cone: the test of end demand is the second half of 2027.

The external transmission, from corporate IT budgets and the PC cycle through distributors to GN's revenue, is clear in direction and in rough size. It is blurred in timing by channel inventory.

C-82

What perception- or sentiment-driven risks are there that are sudden and high-magnitude, including events elsewhere in the sector, theme or market that could hit the share price whatever the company's own fundamentals?

C-3031st of 75

Layer 1 · Risks

Judgement: the sentiment risks are material. GN sits in a sector and industry that Viewforth's environment reading rates Underweight and Double Underweight, in a risk-off regime. It has rallied 21.5% in three months on a catalyst everyone knows. It also has two potential share overhangs. Any of the following could hit the price whatever GN's fundamentals.

  1. A "sell the news" fall after closing and the start of the buyback. The buyback has been the stated attraction since mid-2026 (DNB: "waiting for share buybacks to revitalise investor interest").
  2. The CMD on 24 February 2027 disappointing against the old 5-8% growth and 16-17% margin, if it sets about 3-6% and about 12% or lower.
  3. Read-across from peers' results. - Logitech's guidance and margin pressure from memory costs. - HP and Dell bundling commentary. - The Wisdom Library notes that US bellwethers lead European peers.
  4. A narrative shift towards "software kills the headset".
  5. A further de-rating of consumer discretionary if European consumer readings keep falling. German GfK was -30.8 in November 2026.
  6. Overhangs. - William Demant Invest's roughly 12.3% stake. - The Amplifon shares as lock-ups expire (9-15 months after closing).
  7. History of high short interest. It peaked at 12% of free float in mid-2025, with violent squeezes in both directions.
  8. Optically extreme valuation screens. The 24-month-forward P/E of 22.1x is at the 99th percentile, which can trigger systematic selling even though it is distorted.

C-70

Beyond meeting or beating, what is the record, nature and behaviour of the company and its chief executive in upgrading guidance?

F1571st of 75

Layer 1 · Guidance

Judgement: GN's record of upgrading guidance is weak. From 2022 to 2026 almost every change was a cut. The only "raise", the margin raise of August 2026, was driven by tariff refunds and was an underlying cut of 30bp. The CEO's pattern is to set ambitious, back-end-loaded initial guidance and then narrow or trim it, not to sandbag and beat.

  • Record of changes.
  • Enterprise guidance was cut in 2022, 2023 and 2024.
  • In 2024 organic guidance went from 2-8% at the start of the year to a 1% outcome.
  • In 2025 it went from 3-7% to -2%/+2%, and the outcome was -1%. Margin guidance was cut early to 11-13% on tariffs and landed at 11.4%.
  • In 2026 organic guidance went from 2-8% to 0-6% to 0-3%. The margin guidance went from 8-9% to 9-10%, the raise being refund-driven.
  • In Q3 2026 guidance was confirmed, not raised, despite the beat.
  • Labels in the reports. The change-forces report calls this an "over-promiser archetype". The Wisdom Library's "sandbagging precision management" archetype is the opposite, and GN does not fit it.
  • A possible change. At Q3 the CEO was more tactical and more focused on execution. The CMD is the chance to set beatable targets.
C-69

What is the company's record of missing, meeting or beating guidance and sell-side estimates over the last one, three and five years, including in comparable environmental conditions?

F2471st of 75

Layer 1 · Sell-side estimates

Judgement: over one, three and five years GN has usually missed its initial guidance and estimates on revenue and growth. It has met or beaten on cash flow and cost delivery. In comparable conditions (weak corporate IT, tariffs, a soft consumer: 2022-26) its growth misses were consistent.

  • One year (2025 to Q3 2026).
  • 2025: organic growth missed the initial 3-7% (outcome -1%) and met the narrowed -2%/+2%. The margin missed the 11-13% midpoint (11.4%). Free cash flow beat (DKK 1,112m against DKK 800m).
  • Q1 2026 missed: revenue -4% against consensus, divisional profit -14% against consensus, guidance cut.
  • Q2 2026 was mixed: revenue beat by 1%, EBITA in line, organic guidance cut, margin guidance raised on refunds.
  • Q3 2026 beat: revenue +0.6% against consensus, organic 2.0% against 1.8%, EBITA DKK 413m against DKK 248m (refund-driven; underlying about 5% above).
  • Three years.
  • The 2023 margin of 6.6% was a miss.
  • 2024 organic growth of 1% missed an initial 2-8%.
  • Enterprise guidance was cut in 2022, 2023 and 2024.
  • Five years. 2022 missed (organic -3% against a high-growth target, the SteelSeries debt, supply constraints). 2021 belonged to the COVID audio boom and is not detailed in the files.
  • What this implies. It supports the haircut on revenue guidance set out in the management group judgement.

Judgement: the forecasts should be weighted by line, not as a whole, because GN's record splits cleanly. Revenue forecasts, whether management's, consensus' or mine, deserve low weight and should be leaned down. Cost and capital-return forecasts deserve high weight and should be leaned neutral to slightly up. The adjusted EBITA margin sits in between and should be leaned slightly down from management's informal 10-11% for 2027. In practice I have cut 1.5 points from consensus 2027 revenue growth, 0.4-1.0 points from management's 2027 margin, and nothing from the cost programme or the DKK 4bn return. The record argues for treating any FY2027 revenue guidance on 11 February 2027 as a ceiling, not a midpoint.

Until one full year is delivered against an initial range, the base rate of misses should dominate.

  • FY2026 organic growth within the 0-3% guidance: about 60%, most likely at 0-1%. Adjusted EBITA margin within 9-10%: about 70%, most likely 9.0-9.5% (it needs a Q4 margin of about 11-12% on my revenue).

  • FY2027 adjusted EBITA margin of 10% or more: about 35%. At or above my 9.7%: about 50%.

4. In which direction to lean the 2028-2030 forecasts. Revenue: down, because 2028 must stand on end demand, the weakest-evidenced driver (first set, C-06 and C-10), and the record shows that end demand is where GN errs. Margin: neutral to slightly down; my 11.5% for 2030 already sits below DNB Carnegie's expected CMD target of about 12%. Below-the-line items and share count: up against consensus (P-08), because those are contracted mechanics that some brokers mis-model.

Financial forecasts

Layer 2 · Financial forecasts

Written last by the Investment Analyst for its group

The financial forecasts for RemainCo are dominated by mechanics that are already contracted, and are weakest where GN has always been weakest. Over the next 18 months, the removal of about DKK 600m of interest, a DKK 4bn buyback (about 24% of the market value) and the DKK 200m saving take EPS from about zero to about DKK 4.6 (after PPA amortisation, before special items) with little help from demand. From 2028, about three quarters of EPS growth comes from a share count that roughly halves by 2030, funded first by the Amplifon stake and then by re-levering to the 1.0-1.5x target. Operations add about 10% a year to adjusted EBITA in my base case, from a margin recovery (7.6% underlying in 2026 to about 11.5% in 2030) that is a cyclical rebuild on a high fixed-cost base, not an improvement in the franchise.

For the third set, the predictability question is therefore two-layered:

  1. High predictability: net financial items, share count, the DKK 200m saving, the 2027 capital return. Their main risks are dated and observable: closing (by end-December 2026), the AGM authorisation (about 10 March 2027), Amplifon's share price at the lock-up releases (about September 2027 and March 2028), and accounting choices on development capitalisation at the CMD (24 February 2027).
  2. Low predictability: organic growth and its 30-40% upside and 50-65% downside drop-through, in a business with five years of missed revenue guidance, Europe still not turned, and a Q4 2026 that needs about +5% organic growth and an 11-12% margin just to land at the bottom of guidance.

For the fourth set, the most useful findings are: consensus 2027 EPS is not coherent (brokers' share counts too low, Bernstein's interest too high); consensus capital spending and free cash flow after 2027 are too optimistic; consensus revenue is about 2.5% too high; and the second capital-return wave after 2027 is largely unmodelled. My base case is moderately positive for value per share and neutral for the quality of the business.

2. The share count is the item most likely to be mis-modelled, and in a predictable direction.

  • The mechanics cap how fast the 2027 average can fall. The Safe Harbour buyback is capped at DKK 1bn until about the AGM on 10 March 2027. The DKK 3bn needs AGM authorisation and runs from then to end-2027 at roughly 585,000 shares a day for the first tranche, about 20% of daily volume (DNB Carnegie). At DKK 120-125 a share the time-weighted 2027 average cannot fall much below 125-130m. My base is 129.3m.
  • DNB Carnegie's 2027 EPS (3.81) on net income of about DKK 403m implies about 106m shares. Bernstein uses 114m diluted. Both are closer to a year-end count than an average. Applying a correct average to DNB's net income would cut its 2027 EPS by about 18%.
  • Insight for set four: a correction of brokers' 2027 share counts is a downward force on 2027 EPS consensus that has nothing to do with operations. It is offset by the opposite correction on interest: Bernstein's DKK -461m of 2027 interest is about DKK 0.5bn too heavy. The net direction depends on which brokers revise first.

Per share, it rises from about DKK 3.5 (2027) to about DKK 7.4 (2030).
