P-10

Pulling together all the above answers, in all sets so far, what is your carefully considered base case for the key operational drivers and the key financial line items over the next 18 months to four years?

B+6612th of 75

Layer 2 · Financial forecasts

Judgement: my base case is a cyclical recovery on a cleaned-up, cash-returning balance sheet. Organic growth recovers to about 3.5% a year from 2027. About DKK 10bn is returned over 2027-2030, mostly through buybacks. EPS (after PPA, before special items) goes from about DKK 0.5 (2026) to about 4.6 (2027), 6.3 (2028) and 10.1 (2030). C-78

Beyond cutting costs, how much plausible scope is there to improve the financial outputs over the mid term; do peer precedents show it is achievable; would it come from a few big areas or many small ones; and is it congruent with the stated strategy?

C-6435th of 75

Layer 1 · Company - Economic strength

Judgement: beyond cost, there is plausible scope over the mid-term from a few big areas: volume operating leverage, premium mix including FalCom, the Gaming margin and working capital. Peer precedents show it is achievable in part, and it fits the stated strategy. A realistic mid-term EBITA margin is about 11-13% by 2028-29. The 16-17% reached by Logitech, or the old group target, would require scale or structural change that GN does not have.

  • The areas. 1. Volume. Recovering to about 4% growth a year adds about 1.5-2 points of margin over two years at 40-50% drop-through. 2. Mix. Premium Evolve3 and FalCom. The Q1 Earnings Review suggests FalCom at DKK 300-400m by 2027-28 could add 1.5-2.0 points of margin; that is unconfirmed and optimistic. 3. Gaming. Divisional margin of 11-15% against Logitech's mature peripheral economics. Premium headset share and better mice could add 1-2 points to the division. 4. Working capital release, for cash.
  • Precedents.
  • Logitech's restructuring and premium mix took EBIT margins to about 16%.
  • Demant's DKK 500m programme came in ahead of schedule.
  • Amplifon's Fit4Growth target is 150-200bp.
  • GN's own One GN delivered DKK 600m of synergies.
  • Congruence with strategy. It matches the stated priorities (cost right-sizing, launch cadence, no M&A). DNB's expected CMD target of about 12% EBITA through 2030 sits at the realistic centre.
C-80

To what extent has the core growth engine decelerated over the last twelve months: is the revenue growth rate running below the company's own normal rate, and is the input change force itself slowing?

D4649th of 75

Layer 1 · Risks

Judgement: over the last twelve months the core growth engine has re-accelerated from a deep negative, not decelerated. Growth went from -3% to -5% to -7% to +1% in Enterprise, and from -4% to -4% to +2% for RemainCo. It is still well below GN's normal long-run rate, which DNB puts at about 6% over cycles. The input forces are mixed: the cyclical destocking force is ending, but the structural replacement-cycle force has slowed for good.

  • The input forces.
  • Positive: the end-of-life of the 2020-21 installed base; North American sell-out positive for four quarters; premium Evolve3 demand.
  • Negative and permanent: knowledge-worker headset cycles at 4-5 years (from 2-3); CIO budgets moving to AI software; hot-desking.
  • What to watch. Whether 2027 growth exceeds about 3% once comparisons normalise from H2 2027, and whether Europe follows North America.

P-09

After assessing the other questions/items in the set in greater detail, what is the net likely impact to the main financial line items over the next 18 months to four years, versus the last four to six years?

B+689th of 75

Layer 2 · Financial forecasts

Judgement: the net effect against the last four to six years is a business that is smaller, slower-growing and lower-margin than the old group, but whose per-share figures improve sharply. On my base case, RemainCo revenue grows about 3.5% a year after years of decline; the adjusted EBITA margin recovers from 7.6% to about 11.5%, still below the 15-17% the old Audio business and Logitech achieved; net financial costs fall from DKK 645m to roughly zero and then rise modestly; and the share count roughly halves. EPS is the line that changes most and is the least informative about the business. Revenue and margin are the lines that change least and carry the most risk.

1. The net effect, line by line, against history.

2. What this means.

  • About 70-75% of the EPS change between 2026 and 2030 (0.49 to 10.07, after PPA amortisation) comes from financial mechanics: the share count (about DKK 5.0 a share) and interest removal (about DKK 2.2). These are high-confidence once closing and the AGM have happened. Only about 25-30% (about DKK 2.0-2.4) comes from operations, which carry GN's poor demand record. This is the "leverage-to-EPS transmission" the first set warned about (C-49), now quantified: EPS (model basis) compounds about 26% a year over 2027-2030, net income about 4% and adjusted EBITA about 10%.
  • The business is not getting stronger in my base case. Revenue growth at about market rate assumes flat premium share, a slow European entry-level loss and no structural gain in video or gaming. A margin of 11.5% in 2030 leaves the gap to Logitech at 3-6 points. Return on invested capital stays low because of the SteelSeries goodwill.
  • The risks are concentrated where the change is smallest. A 3-point shortfall in 2027-2028 revenue growth (a European corporate IT freeze) would take about DKK 150-180m off EBITA (about 15-20%), while leaving the per-share bridge intact. That is the case the third set must stress-test.

Memo topic 7, Scope for upgrades over 6 to 12 months: the Rater's score

Behind the rating as a whole

Group judgement

The fourth set as a whole

D4040th of 75

Layer 4 · The fourth set as a whole

Written last by the Investment Analyst for its group

Judgement: the gap between my assessment and what guidance, estimates and the narrative already reflect is modestly unfavourable for the next 6-18 months and roughly balanced beyond. GN is a case in which the market knows the good news and its dates, has priced a large part of it, and has not been given an operating forecast cautious enough to be beaten easily. What remains unreflected in GN's favour is real, but far-dated and dependent on prices GN does not control.

  • What is already reflected.
  • The sale, the net cash, the DKK 4bn and the cost programme are in every broker's model, and in a share price up 21.5% in three months.
  • Consensus 2027 EBITA is at the bottom of the CFO's range, not below it.
  • At about DKK 115-120 a share, after deducting about DKK 50-55 of cash and securities, RemainCo is valued at about 10-11x my 2027 EBITA. That is the upper half of the first set's 8-11x, not a discount.
  • What is not reflected, in the case's favour.
  • A second wave of returns after 2027, modelled by one broker and sized by none. It puts my 2028 earnings per share about 8% above the median.
  • A dividend more than double consensus.
  • Free cash flow a little better than models allow, on leases and tax.
  • A capital-return floor that is more likely to be raised than merely met.
  • What is not reflected, against the case.
  • Revenue about 3% and EBITA about 5-9% below consensus for 2027, with a drop-through that is larger on the way down.
  • A fourth quarter of 2026 likely to meet guidance and still fall short of the named brokers.
  • FY2027 guidance likely to be set near consensus, by a management whose first guidance has been cut in each of the last three years.
  • Share counts in some 2027 estimates that cannot be achieved.
  • A margin target that may be misread, accounting noise from the Amplifon stake, and the end of the transitional agreements in 2028.
  • The sequence the Consumer should expect.
  • To February 2027: closing, the start of the buyback, and mechanical upgrades to earnings per share, which will look like momentum.
  • In February 2027: two operating tests in two weeks, with guidance more likely to be set too high than too low.
  • Through 2027: capital-return news that is good, and operating revisions that lean down.
  • From late 2027: the Amplifon sale and the second programme, the largest under-reflected positive, arriving as the cash floor is used up.
  • What this means for the memo's test that expectations should not capture the probable developments.
  • On capital return, expectations capture the next 12 months fully and the two years after that only partly.
  • On operations, they capture more than I think probable.
  • On a 6-18 month view, the case for owning the shares therefore rests on the support of the buyback and on the chance of an operating surprise in Q4 2026 or at the Capital Markets Day. It does not rest on a gap in expectations.
  • On a three-year view, it rests on per-share compounding that the market under-models, bought at a price that already pays for the first year of it.

I-01

How likely is company guidance to be raised, met or reduced at the next few earnings events (all such events over the next 18 months), and what would drive each outcome? Secondarily, how likely is any guidance, mid-term target or ambition for 18 months to three years out to be raised, met or reduced?

F3664th of 75

Layer 4 · Guidance

Judgement: over the next 18 months operating guidance is more likely to be reduced than raised, and capital-return guidance is more likely to be raised than reduced. FY2026 guidance will probably be met at the bottom of both ranges, with close to an even chance that organic growth falls just short of the 0% floor. The first FY2027 guidance, on 11 February 2027, will probably be set around consensus, which is above my forecast. I put the chance that it is lowered, or narrowed downwards, during 2027 at about 50%, against about 10% that it is raised. The "at least DKK 4bn" is the one piece of guidance set conservatively against my assessment.

1. FY2026 guidance (organic growth of 0-3%; adjusted EBITA margin of 9-10%), reported on 11 February 2027.

Below the range Within the range Above the range
Organic growth about 45% about 50%, at 0-1% about 5%
Adjusted EBITA margin about 25% about 70%, at 9.0-9.4% about 5%
  • Growth.
  • After -2% over nine months, the year reaches 0% only if Q4 grows about 5%.
  • Bernstein's Q4 assumptions (Enterprise -1%, Gaming +15%) give about +4%: just short. Mine (Enterprise about +2% after a FalCom drag of 2.5 points, Gaming about +12%) give about +5%: just enough.
  • For Gaming: the release of Grand Theft Auto 6, retailers' accelerated holiday orders (Turtle Beach, Corsair) and Logitech's supply shortfall of USD 70-80m.
  • Against Enterprise: soft PC demand, and DNB Carnegie's expectation of flat growth.
  • Margin.
  • A 9.0% year needs Q4 EBITA of about DKK 320m (11.0%). I have DKK 340m.
  • Seasonal promotions are heavy. DNB Carnegie expects Gaming's gross margin to fall to 35% in Q4, from 41% underlying in Q3. Logitech guides its own gross margin down 2 points.
  • No refunds remain to be booked: the DKK 153m taken in Q3 is already above the guided DKK 100-150m.
  • A point for the estimates (I-02). DNB Carnegie and Bernstein carry FY2026 adjusted EBITA of DKK 944m and DKK 951m. Those need about DKK 415-420m in Q4, a 14% margin. Last year the fourth quarter added DKK 561m of revenue over the third, and only about DKK 47m of EBITA. A result that meets guidance at 9.2% would be about 8% below those two brokers.
  • Raised: no longer possible in substance. A pre-announcement in January: about 25%, most likely if growth is below 0%.

2. The first FY2027 guidance (11 February 2027).

  • What I expect.
  • A growth range about four points wide. Its midpoint: 4% or higher (about 55% probable), 3-4% (about 35%), lower (about 10%).
  • A margin range centred on the CFO's 10-11% EBITA, probably restated as EBITDA of about 12-13% on the company's definition.
  • Why not lower.
  • The company's initial growth ranges were 2-8%, 3-7% and 2-8% in the last three years.
  • Consensus is about 5% growth and a 10% EBITA margin.
  • The CEO's informal commentary speaks of a "clear turnaround in commercial traction".
  • Guiding below consensus two weeks before a Capital Markets Day would undercut the event.
  • Why it might be lower. The CEO's tone at Q3 was "more tactical". The 2026 growth range had to be cut twice. A new company has every reason to begin with a bar it can clear. A range of 1-5% would put my forecast at its midpoint.
  • Against my forecast (organic growth of about 3%, an EBITA margin of 9.4%). The likely guidance has my growth in its lower half and my margin at or just below its floor.

3. Through 2027 (Q1 on 29 April, Q2 in August, Q3 in November).

FY2027 guidance Raised Kept and met Lowered or narrowed downwards
Organic growth about 10% about 40% about 50%
Margin about 10% about 50% about 40%
  • When a cut would come.
  • At Q1: about 15%. Comparisons are easiest and the savings arrive, but any pay-back from the channel falls here.
  • At Q2: about 25%. This is the point at which the second half must be owned, and the point at which cuts were made in 2025 and 2026.
  • At Q3: about 10%.
  • What would drive a cut. Europe flat instead of up; PC and peripheral budgets as now forecast; mid-tier Evolve3 not selling through; gaming promotions once Logitech's supply returns in early 2027; a hard fourth-quarter comparison in Gaming.
  • What would drive a raise. Europe following North America; a gaming upgrade cycle when Grand Theft Auto 6 reaches PCs; lower memory prices in the second half; or one-off gains. The last is the likeliest route for the margin. GN counts tariff refunds inside adjusted EBITA, and the only raise of 2026 came that way.
  • What "met" would rest on. A wide range, and first-half comparisons that put much of the year's growth in the bank by August.

4. Capital-return guidance ("at least DKK 4bn from closing to end-2027").

  • By February 2028: raised or extended about 65%; met as stated about 30%; fallen short about 5%.
  • Why raised.
  • The wording is a floor.
  • Net cash after closing, a year's cash flow and the first Amplifon tranche (about DKK 1.3bn from about September 2027) cover DKK 4bn without borrowing.
  • The gearing target of 1.0-1.5x implies about DKK 5.5-6.5bn more over 2028-2030.
  • Management has said the Amplifon proceeds will be distributed. The Capital Markets Day is the natural place to say how.
  • Why short. Closing slipping into 2027, or the authorisation being scaled back after a market fall.

5. Secondary: the mid-term targets from the Capital Markets Day, and their fate over 18 months to three years.

  • Expected: organic growth of 3-6% a year and a margin of "about 12%" to 2030.
  • The definition decides the answer.
  • If the 12% is EBITA, my path (10.3% in 2028, 11.3% in 2030) falls short. Reduced or abandoned: about 35%. Met at the low end: about 50%. Raised: about 15%.
  • If the 12% is EBITDA on the company's definition, it is an EBITA margin of about 10%, which my forecast passes in 2028. Reduced: about 15%. Met: about 65%. Raised: about 20%.
  • Growth. My 3.5% is at the low end of 3-6%. The targets set in 2024 lasted less than two years.
  • How the market may receive it. Either reading is far below the suspended 16-17% EBITA margin and 5-8% growth. An EBITDA target of 12% would read as low ambition on the day, and would be the more beatable bar afterwards.

6. The gap, for the investment case.

  • On operations, guidance is likely to be set above what I think probable, by a management whose guidance the market already treats with caution. That is unfavourable, though partly expected.
  • On capital return, guidance understates what the balance sheet and the gearing target imply. That is favourable, and it is the kind of guidance this management has kept.

2. The next three months: closing, then 11 and 24 February 2027.

Line Higher Held Lower Why
2027 earnings per share, median about 45% about 30% about 25% Late updates for interest lift the low end (Bernstein; Sydbank at DKK 1.55 since August; two entries at DKK 2.60-3.06). Share-count corrections pull the high end down

  • Breadth of 2027 earnings-per-share revisions: positive. More brokers have interest to remove than shares to add back.
  • The pattern of the last three months should continue. 2027 earnings per share are up 12%, EBITDA up 5% and sales unchanged, while the share of buy ratings is down 6 points to 29%. Estimates rise for mechanical reasons while ratings fall on valuation. DNB Carnegie's latest change shows the mechanism in a single note: it raised 2026 earnings per share by 7% and cut 2027 pre-tax profit by 13%.
  • A warning on the dashboard's rating. The sell-side momentum rating of D may improve on earnings-per-share and target-price revisions without any improvement in operating expectations. The Consumer should not read such an improvement as a fundamental signal.

3. Months 3 to 18: the 2027 quarters, and the FY2027 results in February 2028.

Line Higher Held Lower
2027 revenue about 15% about 25% about 60%
2027 adjusted EBITA about 15% about 30% about 55%
2027 earnings per share about 25% about 30% about 45%
2028 earnings per share about 50% about 20% about 30%
  • Operating lines down. My revenue is 3% lower, and the drop-through on a shortfall is 50-60%. The cuts are most likely at the Q2 and Q3 2027 reports, when the easy comparisons have passed. Each would probably be small: brokers have left 2027 sales unchanged for three months and tend to wait for guidance to move.
  • 2027 earnings per share drifting down. By then the operating cuts are larger than any remaining mechanical gains. One correction is still to come. Full-year averages of 106-114m shares cannot be reached when DKK 3bn of the DKK 4bn is bought after 10 March 2027, at about 20% of daily volume. DNB Carnegie itself notes that the full benefit arrives only in 2028.
  • 2028 earnings per share up. Most brokers carry no 2028 programme. One will be added to models when the company describes it: at the Capital Markets Day, or when the first Amplifon shares are sold from about September 2027. DNB Carnegie, which already includes DKK 3bn in 2028, is at DKK 6.84, above my DKK 6.16. The median should move into the range between the two.
  • Other revisions I expect.
  • Capital spending up and free cash flow down for 2028-2029, unless the company announces lower development spending.
  • Dividends up, once the 20-40% policy is applied to a normal year.
  • A new line for the Amplifon stake, once its accounting is known.

4. Eighteen months to three years out (2028-2029).

  • Revenue: lower about 55%, held about 30%, higher about 15%. Consensus growth of 4.4% and 4.0% needs end demand that the evidence does not yet support.
  • EBITA margin: consensus implies about 10.4-11.0% in 2028 and about 11-12% in 2029 (the latter from one or two brokers), against my 10.3% and 10.9%. Lower is somewhat more likely than higher.
  • Earnings per share: the single 2029 figure of DKK 8.65 is not a consensus. As coverage fills in, the outcome will depend more on each broker's buyback assumptions than on operations.

5. What would make me wrong.

  • Upgrades instead of cuts. Enterprise growing in Q4 2026 despite FalCom; a strong holiday quarter in Gaming that carries into Q1 2027; guidance set with room to be raised. In that case the operating gearing works for the estimates: each point of revenue above consensus adds about 4% to EBITA.
  • Deeper cuts. The downside case of D-04, in which 2027 EBITA would be about 25-30% below consensus.

6. The gap, for the investment case. Consensus does not contain an operating recovery that I think too cautious. If anything, the reverse. What consensus under-reflects is confined to the per-share mechanics of 2028 and beyond, and to the dividend. Near-term revision momentum may look better than it is.

11d

Current multiple

D3263rd of 79Memo topic 11, The fair multiple: the Rater's score1 answer
D-0762
D-07

To what extent do the share price and the multiple move with sector and industry peers rather than idiosyncratically (on a rolling six-month basis), and so how far is the multiple company-driven rather than industry-driven?

A623rd of 75

Layer 3 · Risks

Judgement: the share price and the multiple are almost wholly company-driven at present. Over the latest six months the peer basket explains 8% of GN's daily price variance; 92% is GN's own. The multiple's move from about 10x to 22x two-year-forward earnings during 2026 was produced by GN's own change of perimeter and balance sheet, not by any re-rating of the sector. I expect this to last until the capital events are complete in early 2028, with one addition. After closing GN will hold Amplifon shares worth about a quarter of its own market value, and its price should begin to move with Amplifon's.

1. The evidence.

  • The latest window (1 June to 24 November 2026). Correlation with the equal-weighted basket of 17 sector peers is 0.29, beta 0.68 and R-squared 8%. GN rose 17.3% while the basket rose 2.7%. No single peer explains more than 7%.
  • Over 24 months. R-squared rose to about 50% between April and July 2025, with a beta of about 1.75, when US tariffs hit every consumer hardware company at once. It fell to 12-16% by late 2025 and to 4-6% from March 2026, the month the Hearing sale was announced.
  • The pattern. GN moves with its sector when one external shock dominates, and alone when corporate events dominate. When it does move with the sector it moves about 1.7 times as far.

2. Two cautions on the measure.

  • The basket is the dashboard's Consumer products sector: household appliances, outdoor equipment, toys and similar. It contains none of GN's operating peers (Logitech, Corsair, Turtle Beach, HP). A low reading against it shows independence from European consumer-goods shares, not necessarily from the PC-peripheral cycle. The files hold no co-movement against Logitech, which is the comparison the Consumer most needs.
  • The table measures price, not the multiple. The multiple's own history shows the same thing more strongly. The two-year-forward P/E is at the 99th percentile of five years because consensus earnings were re-based for the smaller, cash-rich company while the price rose 21.5% in three months on the buyback.

3. What drives the multiple from here.

  • To early 2028: company events.
  • About 45% of the share price is cash and securities that will be turned into fewer shares.
  • The price should respond to closing, to the pace and price of the buyback, to the two operating tests of February 2027 and to Amplifon's share price.
  • The Amplifon stake is about a quarter of GN's market value at closing, so a 10% move in Amplifon is worth about 2.5% of GN's share price until the stake is sold.
  • From 2028: the industry. Once the cash has been returned GN is a geared, single-industry hardware company. Its multiple should then follow the PC-peripheral and gaming cycle and Logitech's rating. Its beta to its sector should return towards the 1.7 seen in 2025.

4. What this means for predictability and for the case.

  • Favourable. Viewforth's environment reading rates the sector Underweight and the industry Double Underweight, in a risk-off regime (C-07). A share that takes 8% of its movement from that sector is largely insulated from it for now. The return depends on GN's own, dated events.
  • Unfavourable. Company-driven means concentrated. The results on 11 February and the Capital Markets Day on 24 February 2027 fall within two weeks of each other, on a share that has risen sharply into them. And the insulation is temporary: it lasts as long as the cash does.

Outside the eleven ratings

Answers not placed under any of the eleven ratings

The table the ratings map uses places these nowhere; each is shown with the reason the table records.

Why it is not placed It asks whether the stock fits the sector's historical patterns of share price and financial outperformance, a cross-check across many topics rather than one of the eleven.

C-84

What do historical precedents, rules of thumb and the empirical evidence show about how companies in this sector and industry have typically generated share price, financial and operational outperformance, and underperformance, and to what extent does this company fit those patterns?

C5228th of 75

Layer 1 · Industry and Value Chain

Judgement: the precedents show that in this sector and industry, share-price outperformance comes from (1) volume-led growth, not price, (2) a maker's channels finishing destocking before its peers', and (3) cash returned from a shrinking asset base. Underperformance comes from leverage, misses against guidance, and being the subscale specialist against a bundler. GN currently fits the first set of outperformance patterns in the near term, partly already priced, and the underperformance patterns in the mid-term.

Rules from the digests.

  • Sector (Consumer products).
  • "The market keeps paying a high multiple while volume grows, not only price", and "a worry that growth is led by price can pull such a share price back."
  • "When shops finish cutting stock, a maker's sales rise back to what households buy ... the order in which channels finish cutting sets the order of the makers' recoveries."
  • "Higher interest rates keep shops' stocks permanently lower ... no restocking boom."
  • A maker's multiple rises once its customers finish destocking (Mips).
  • Industry (Consumer discretionary).
  • "Cash returned from a shrinking asset base needs no recovery in demand, but read the figure net of the claims on it."
  • "A worry clearing can move a share price furthest and fastest, and is the hardest to hold."
  • "At trough earnings the P/E misleads."

Peer precedents.

  • Logitech. After the post-pandemic bust in FY2022-23, it was re-rated on cost discipline, protected gross margins (about 45-50%) and buybacks backed by net cash, and now earns a 14-18% EBIT margin. This is the operating template the bulls hold up for GN. Logitech had about 3x GN's revenue scale.
  • Plantronics/Poly. It bought Polycom on debt in 2018, lost share, and was sold to HP in 2022 at a depressed value. This is the template for a leveraged, subscale number two.
  • GN itself. The SteelSeries leverage and repeated misses took the shares down about 80% from their post-COVID peak. The 20% rally on deal day showed how quickly the market rewards balance-sheet repair.
  • Sonova's 2026 exit from Sennheiser consumer audio is evidence that non-enterprise audio has challenging margins and heavy promotion.

How GN fits.

  • Positive near term: North American channels are finishing destocking first; DKK 4bn of cash return is dated; trough earnings make the P/E misleading. The shares are up 21.5% in three months, so part of this is already priced.
  • Negative mid term: the subscale specialist facing bundlers; an over-promiser on guidance; and Q3's growth, which was partly price-assisted and recovery-from-trough rather than clean volume growth.

For the model and set four: the sustainable re-rating needs evidence of volume growth in 2027 H2 and 2028, not only the buyback.

Why it is not placed The table holds this question in reserve for Riskiness sub-items that are not yet built, so it is not yet placed.

C-83

How far could the shares fall to a fair trough multiple?

C-4833rd of 75

Layer 1 · Risks

Judgement: on my reconciliation, a fair trough is about DKK 75-90 a share, 22-35% below DKK 114.9. That combines a trough multiple of about 6-7x EV/EBITA on trough RemainCo EBITA of about DKK 0.75bn with non-operating assets marked down. Non-operating value of roughly DKK 35-60 a share, depending mostly on Amplifon's share price, anchors a floor. The rest of the price, about DKK 55-80 a share, is the cyclical RemainCo, which is where the risk sits.

Build (my estimates, not sourced figures).

Component Assumption Value
Trough RemainCo EBITA ~DKK 0.75bn, close to the FY2025 pro-forma DKK 727m and the 2026 underlying run-rate
Trough multiple 6-7x EV/EBITA: below Bernstein's and Jefferies' 10x EV/EBIT and the 33-50% discounts to Logitech that brokers apply EV ~DKK 4.5-5.3bn, ~DKK 31-36 a share
Net cash excluding Amplifon ~DKK 2.5-4bn ~DKK 17-27 a share
Amplifon stake ~DKK 3-4.4bn, stressed by -20% to -30% ~DKK 15-24 a share
Nations Benefits DKK 0.5-1.5bn ~DKK 3-10 a share
Total roughly DKK 66-97; central range DKK 75-90

Cross-checks.

  • Jefferies' bear case is DKK 68 and Morgan Stanley's DKK 79. Bernstein's target of DKK 79.15 predates the tax cut, which adds roughly DKK 7-11 a share.
  • The 52-week low is DKK 85.12.
  • The dashboard's "crash" band, the 10th percentile of the 10-year P/E at 9.2x on 24-month-forward EPS of DKK 5.44, gives about DKK 50. That method misleads here because it ignores the cash.

Buybacks. Done above trough prices, they reduce cash per share. Done below intrinsic value, they raise value per share.

Why it is not placed It rates the staircase of news the market can look forward to, and none of the eleven rates the narrative or the catalysts as such.

Group judgement

Stock market narrative

A566th of 75

Layer 1 · Stock market narrative

Written last by the Investment Analyst for its group

The narrative staircase is rich, dated and front-loaded with high-certainty capital events. Closing, at least DKK 4bn of buybacks, a reinstated dividend, a CMD, and probably a second wave from Amplifon proceeds. That gives the market a steady stream of positive news for 12-18 months, and the shares a technical bid from the buyback (about 6% of shares by March 2027 and over 20% by end-2027 at current prices).

The weakness is novelty and content. The capital steps are known and partly priced. The operating steps that would change the market's view of RemainCo, namely sustained volume growth, Europe turning and the margin moving towards 12%, are less certain. They are also judged by an audience that has watched five years of missed guidance.

The next six months carry two binary operating tests:

  1. Q4 2026 and FY2027 guidance on 11 February 2027.
  2. The CMD on 24 February 2027.

Each can extend or break the staircase. For the Consumer, the narrative supports forecasting the share count falling quickly and the capital return being delivered. It does not by itself support raising the operating forecasts.

Why it is not placed It rates how well the earlier answers agree with each other, which is a check on the analysis rather than on the company.

D-01

How congruent are the answers to all the questions in the earlier sets: to what extent do they support and align with each other, rather than contradict or jar?

–82

Layer 3 · Risks

Judgement: the first two sets are highly congruent in structure and conclusion, and the new research strengthens that structure. Every strand, written from different evidence, arrives at the same two-layer case: a near-certain financial bridge (debt repaid, shares retired) sitting on an uncertain, cyclical operating business, run by a management that delivers on cost and capital and misses on demand. The contradictions are of degree and of fact, not of direction. Six matter. Their net effect is to make the operating comparison with consensus less favourable and the balance-sheet and cash figures slightly more favourable.

1. Where the sets support each other.

  • The split between what GN controls and what it does not appears independently in four places: management's record (C-61, C-69, P-06), the specificity of guidance (C-72), the sources of earnings growth (C-49, P-09) and the staircase of events (C-22). The new research reports reach the same split by their own route. Each rates the evidence on debt, buybacks and cost "high" confidence and the evidence on demand and the channel "low" or "medium".
  • Growth and price. The sector precedents (C-84), the competitive analysis (C-27, C-35) and the forecasts (P-04, P-10) agree that RemainCo's growth is market growth with flat share at best, and that price defends margin without expanding it.
  • Valuation. The first set's implied 7-10x EBITA for RemainCo, the sell-side's falling share of buy ratings and the second set's below-consensus operating forecasts all say that the known positives are largely in the price.

2. Where they jar, and my ruling.

Tension First set Second set Ruling on the evidence
Does consensus discount management's margin? Consensus EBIT of 7.0% sits below the CFO's 10-11%, partly on credibility (C-68) Its own forecast is 6-12% below management Consensus EBITA is about 10.0%: no discount. We are about 5-9% below consensus, not alongside it
Do the 2026 impairments help 2027 profit? Yes, a "big bath" tailwind (C-20, C-54) No, amortisation is rising (P-03) The second set is right: DKK 91m, 98m, 100m a quarter
Is capitalisation being stretched? Capitalisation has been cut to 53% (C-14) 72% in Q3 2026, a reporting risk (P-03) The first set is right: 58% in Q3, 52% over nine months
How wide is the earnings-per-share cone? Not addressed "Much narrower" than the EBITA cone (P-01, P-10) I disagree with the second set (D-04). The level shift is locked in; the range around it is not
Is the buyback an unqualified positive? Yes, score 80 (C-19) Qualified by the price paid and by Amplifon (P-05) The second set is right. The first set's own test of management (C-64) says the same
Value of the Amplifon stake DKK 3-4.4bn DKK 3.45bn About DKK 4.7bn at market; I use DKK 4.0bn

3. Two inconsistencies neither set noticed.

  • The floor and the buyback are the same money.
  • The first set's fair trough of DKK 75-90 rests on DKK 35-60 a share of non-operating assets (C-83). The second set's forecasts spend all of it on buybacks by 2028 and then borrow about DKK 2.5bn more by 2030 (P-02).
  • Both cannot hold at once beyond about 15 months. The floor protects the holder while the cash is being turned into fewer shares. After that the holder owns a geared claim on a cyclical business, and the floor is whatever that business is worth.
  • The two sets are congruent only if the Consumer dates the claim: a low-risk case to early 2028, a cyclical equity after it.
  • The stated caution and the model's output. The second set's operating forecasts sit below consensus. The model built from them shows forecasts far above consensus and a threefold return. That is the base-year fault described above, not a view. It is nonetheless the largest incongruence in the files the Consumer holds.

4. Why this matters for predictability. When independent strands of evidence agree on which parts of a forecast are firm and which are soft, the forecast can be relied on in proportion: firmly for interest and share count, loosely for revenue. The corrections above change no sign. They do remove one comfort: that consensus was already cautious on margin.

Why it is not placed It rates how well the sell-side and investors already articulate the case, and none of the eleven rates the narrative or how much of the case is known.

I-03

How comprehensively are these dynamics articulated in the current narrative of (i) sell-side commentary and (ii) investor commentary (for example on Twitter): how well known is each of them to investors who own or cover the company or its sector, primarily for the next 18 months and secondarily for 18 months to three years out?

C4629th of 75

Judgement: the narrative is comprehensive on the events and thin on their mechanics. Everything that is positive and dated (the sale, the net cash, the DKK 4bn, the cost programme) is known to every broker and, on the limited evidence available, is what investors talk about. The dynamics that decide how much those events are worth per share, and the dynamics that will shape 2028-2030, are discussed by one or two brokers or by none. Most of what is under-discussed is neutral or negative for the next 6-12 months, and positive only further out. The evidence on investor commentary, as distinct from sell-side commentary, is very thin: the research found no usable material from Twitter or investor forums beyond a general focus on the buyback.

4. Investor commentary.

  • The research was asked for commentary on Twitter and investor forums, and found almost none it could cite. Its one finding is that forum commentators "ignore the bundling threat, focusing entirely on the impending share buyback programme".
  • Indirect evidence agrees. The shares rose 21.5% in three months, to the 83rd percentile of relative strength, while sales estimates did not move. Brokers report investors across hardware shares weighing buybacks over margin risk.
  • I conclude, with low confidence, that the investor narrative is the first table above, reduced to its first row. The memo should say that this conclusion rests on thin evidence.

5. Primarily the next 18 months; secondarily beyond.

  • The next 18 months. The positives are in the narrative. What is missing is mostly unhelpful: estimates without a cushion, a cone skewed to the downside, share counts to be corrected, a possible pay-back quarter, accounting noise. The one under-discussed positive inside the period is the likelihood that the capital return is raised or extended.
  • Eighteen months to three years. The under-discussed items cut both ways, and are larger. On one side: a second wave of returns of DKK 5.5-6.5bn against a market value of about DKK 17bn. On the other: the end of the transitional agreements, the re-gearing of a cyclical business, and consensus capital spending that cannot be reconciled with its margin path.

Each layer's own material

What each layer was asked, what its scores mean, and the Investment Analyst's notes and appendices

Layer 1: Assessing the foundational components of an investment case

Written 23 November 2026, 11:14 to 11:36 UK, Opus, main login

What the Investment Analyst was asked

The objective is to investigate and research each question, analyse the relevant information and form an insightful judgement regarding it, and to do the same for each group of questions as a whole, so as to best help the Consumer achieve their objective, which is to produce a well-reasoned, coherent, deeply understood and accurate operating model and financial model for the company for the next 18 months to four years.

What the scores mean in this layer

Finish each answer and each group judgement with a score from 1 to 100; a higher number is more positive for an investment case in the company.

For this set of questions the score says how positive the thing the question asks about is for an investment case in the company: 100 the most positive, 1 the most negative, 50 neither.

The Investment Analyst's title
GN Store Nord (GN-DK): answers to the first set of questions, as at 23 November 2026

The Investment Analyst's opening notes

Orientation for the Consumer: the perimeter, the modelling base and the data cautions

These facts underpin every answer below. They are set out once here so that the answers can be read against them.

  1. The perimeter to model. GN agreed on 16 March 2026 to sell its Hearing division (ReSound, Beltone) to Amplifon for DKK 17.0bn on a cash-free, debt-free basis: DKK 12.6bn in cash plus 56 million Amplifon shares, worth DKK 4.4bn at announcement (about 16% of Amplifon). The European Commission cleared the deal on 6 November 2026, the company announced the same day that all regulatory approvals had been met, and closing is expected by end-2026. Hearing is reported as discontinued. The company to model, which these answers call "RemainCo", is Enterprise (Jabra office headsets, video collaboration, BlueParrott, FalCom tactical communications) and Gaming (SteelSeries). The internal demerger was completed on 1 November 2026.
  2. The latest Earnings Review is confirmed. It covers Q3 2026, the three months to 30 September 2026, reported on 5 November 2026. That matches the last period FactSet shows (09/2026). The next results are due on 11 February 2027.
  3. The FY2025 base (continuing operations, company definitions). - Revenue was DKK 9,568m: Enterprise 6,899m and Gaming 2,669m. - Adjusted EBITA was DKK 727m, a 7.6% margin. That figure already carries about DKK 200m of costs stranded by the Hearing carve-out. The gross margin was 49.6%. - Divisional profit was DKK 2,311m in Enterprise (33.5%) and DKK 310m in Gaming (11.6%). - The shared costs that sit below divisional profit (R&D, administration and central functions) were therefore about DKK 1.9bn, roughly 20% of revenue. This largely fixed block is the source of RemainCo's operating leverage.
  4. Quarterly base, continuing operations (DKKm, revenue / adjusted EBITA / margin). Q2 and Q4 2025 EBITA are derived from the reported margin and full-year total. - 2025: Q1 2,283 / 129 / 5.7%; Q2 2,302 / about 163 / 7.1%; Q3 2,211 / 194 / 8.8%; Q4 2,772 / about 241 / about 8.7%. - 2026: Q1 2,096 / 6 / 0.3% (organic -4%); Q2 2,171 / 110 / 5.1% (organic -4%); Q3 2,267 / 413 / 18.2% reported (organic +2.0%). - Q3 2026 underlying was 11.5% (about DKK 260m) once the DKK 153m US tariff refund booked in cost of goods sold is excluded. That 11.5% was itself flattered by a correction of over-allocated carve-out general and administrative costs; the CFO told investors to use the first-three-quarter average as the run-rate. - Nine months of 2026: revenue 6,534 (-3.9% against 6,796) and adjusted EBITA 529 reported or about 376 underlying (5.8%), against 486 (7.2%) a year earlier. - Caution: one Q1 review table shows Q3 and Q4 2025 divisional revenues that do not sum to the restated 9,568. Use Q3 2025 = 2,211 and Q4 2025 = 2,772, which do sum.
  5. FY2026 guidance and what it implies for Q4. - Organic growth guidance is 0% to 3%, cut twice (from 2-8% in February to 0-6% in May, then to 0-3% in August). - The adjusted EBITA margin guidance is 9% to 10%, raised in August from 8-9% purely on US tariff refunds of DKK 100-150m. Bernstein calculates that, excluding the refunds, the midpoint fell by 30bp. - With DKK 153m already booked and FactSet revenue consensus at DKK 9,510m, the range implies Q4 2026 EBITA of about DKK 330-420m on about DKK 2.98bn of revenue. That is an 11-14% margin, against about 8.7% in Q4 2025, with organic growth of about +5-6% needed in Q4 despite a FalCom drag Bernstein puts at -2.5 points.
  6. Balance-sheet bridge. - Net interest-bearing debt was DKK 9,578m at Q3 2026 (3.9x). - At closing GN receives DKK 12.6bn of cash. The upfront tax payment is now DKK 300-500m (the prior estimate was DKK 1.5-2.0bn). Because the payment creates an equal-sized future tax asset, the future tax shield shrinks with it. - One-off cash carve-out costs are DKK 750m across 2026-27, about 75% of it in 2026. - The company and DNB Carnegie give net cash after closing of DKK 5-6bn. DNB separately projects net cash of about DKK 2bn in early 2027, "excluding the upcoming share buybacks". - My own crude bridge, excluding the Amplifon shares, gives roughly DKK 2.5-4bn. The difference most plausibly lies in whether the Amplifon shares are counted, lease liabilities that transfer with Hearing, and Q4 cash flow. This must be reconciled before the Consumer fixes the opening balance sheet.
  7. Capital return framework. - At least DKK 4bn is to be distributed from closing to end-2027: a Safe Harbour buyback of up to DKK 1bn from closing to March 2027, plus DKK 3bn to be authorised at the 2027 AGM. - A dividend policy of 20-40% of net profit has been reinstated (the old policy was 15-25%, and nothing has been paid since FY2021). - The gearing target is 1.0x to 1.5x net debt to EBITDA, so the company intends to carry some debt again. - The Amplifon shares are under a staggered 9-15 month lock-up and will be sold down over time to fund distributions. GN also holds a 19% stake in Nations Benefits (DNB: DKK 1.5bn). - There are 145.6m shares outstanding (150.9m issued, about 3.5% held in treasury). DKK 4bn at DKK 115-125 a share retires about 32-35m shares, roughly 22-24% of the count.
  8. Consensus (FactSet median at 19 November 2026; thin coverage: 5-7 brokers, 1-2 for 2029). - Revenue (2026-29, DKKm): 9,510 / 9,974 / 10,412 / 10,827. - EBIT: 145 / 699 / 901 / 1,077. - EPS (DKK): 0.05 / 3.90 / 5.68 / 8.65. - Consensus EBIT for 2027 is a 7.0% margin, against the CFO's "underlying EBITA run-rate of 10-11% in 2027" (Q1 call, 7 May 2026). The gap is part definition (amortisation of acquired intangibles, one-offs) and part credibility. The median lines come from different broker sets and are internally inconsistent, so they must not be used as one coherent model.
  9. Estimate-revision trap. The 6- and 12-month cuts to FY2027 consensus (sales -45%/-47%, EPS -60%/-65%) are mainly the reclassification of Hearing as discontinued in May 2026, not a fundamental downgrade. The fundamental signal is the last three months: EPS +12%, EBITDA +5%, sales flat, target price +8%.
  10. Market and implied valuation.
    • The price was DKK 114.9 on 20 November 2026. The 52-week range is 85.12-130.5, the shares are up 21.5% over three months, and relative strength is at the 83rd percentile.
    • The 24-month-forward P/E of 22.1x sits at the 99th percentile of five years. That reading means little on trough EPS and a cash-rich balance sheet.
    • At DKK 114.9 the market capitalisation is about DKK 16.7bn. Subtracting non-operating assets (net cash excluding Amplifon of about DKK 2.5-4bn, the Amplifon stake at roughly DKK 3-4.4bn, Nations Benefits at about DKK 1-1.5bn) implies a RemainCo enterprise value of about DKK 7-10bn.
    • That is about 7-10x a 10% 2027 EBITA (about DKK 1.0bn), or 10-14x consensus 2027 EBIT. On this reconciliation RemainCo is priced at a modest discount to listed peers, not a deep one, which differs from the sell-side's "deep discount" framing.

Appendix Research requests the Investment Analyst raised for Layer 2

Research requests for the next set

Company: GN Store Nord A/S (GN-DK), a Danish maker of Jabra enterprise headsets, video collaboration devices and FalCom tactical communication headsets (Enterprise division) and of SteelSeries gaming headsets, keyboards, mice and software (Gaming division). It agreed on 16 March 2026 to sell its Hearing division to Amplifon, with closing expected by end-2026.

Request: T-P1

Peers: Logitech International (closest listed peer in enterprise video, headsets and gaming peripherals; cost reset and margin recovery after the post-pandemic downturn); HP Inc. with its Poly business, formerly Plantronics (number two in enterprise headsets; Plantronics' debt-funded Polycom acquisition, cost programmes and sale to HP in 2022); Corsair Gaming (listed gaming-peripheral maker with volatile margins through product cycles); Turtle Beach (listed gaming-headset maker; restructuring precedent); Razer (private since 2022; gaming-peripheral rival); Cisco Systems (leader in meeting-room video); Yealink Network Technology (listed low-cost entrant in enterprise headsets and video); EPOS (enterprise headset business restructured by Demant and sold to ACCO Brands in January 2026); Sonova (exit from Sennheiser Consumer Hearing, for consumer-audio cost economics); Demant (DKK 500 million cost programme announced February 2026, a Danish restructuring precedent). Inputs: (1) The split of fixed and variable costs in GN's continuing Enterprise and Gaming operations, using the company's divisional-profit and shared-cost disclosures: FY2025 continuing revenue DKK 9,568 million, adjusted EBITA DKK 727 million, Enterprise divisional profit DKK 2,311 million and Gaming divisional profit DKK 310 million (Annual Report 2025; Interim Report Q1 2026). (2) How adjusted EBITA moved with revenue in each quarter from 2024 to Q3 2026 for continuing operations, including the 0.3% margin in Q1 2026 and the 18.2% margin in Q3 2026 that included a DKK 153 million US tariff refund (company interim reports), and what explains the drop-through each quarter. (3) What GN's past cost programmes delivered against what was promised and how long they took: the One GN integration (about DKK 600 million of synergies by 2026), the 2024 wind-down of the Elite and Talk consumer lines, and the 2025 relocation of 31 production lines out of China. (4) Exactly which cost lines the DKK 200 million run-rate cost programme cuts (announced with Q1 2026 results on 6-7 May 2026; full effect in 2027): administration, the finance service centre in Warsaw, about 300 roles. How much is reinvested, and how does it relate to the DKK 200 million of stranded costs left by the Hearing carve-out and the DKK 750 million of one-off cash costs in 2026-2027? (5) What the CFO meant by the cost initiatives supporting underlying margins "with around 2% in 2027" (Q1 2026 earnings call, 7 May 2026), and his statement that Q3 2026 general and administrative costs benefited from a correction of carve-out cost allocations (Q3 2026 earnings call, 5 November 2026). (6) GN's administration costs and R&D as shares of sales against Logitech's and Corsair's, the share of GN's development spending that is capitalised (DKK 1,034 million capitalised in 2025, Annual Report 2025) against Logitech's policy of expensing all R&D, and how the DKK 1.3 billion of non-cash impairments booked in 2026 changes future amortisation. (7) How cuttable GN's working capital is against peers' (receivables, inventory, payables, distributor terms), given that a single distributor generated DKK 2,001 million of GN sales in 2025 (Annual Report 2025). (8) How Logitech's, Plantronics' or Poly's, and Corsair's operating margins moved in the 2020-2021 upswing and the 2022-2024 downswing, which cost lines proved cuttable, and which did not. (9) Which costs and scale benefits GN loses or gains by separating from Hearing: purchasing volumes, shared IT, and transitional service agreements with Amplifon. Focus: How much of the gap between GN's continuing operating margin and Logitech's is overhead that can realistically be removed after the Hearing separation, and how much EBITA moves for each percentage point of revenue change once the DKK 200 million programme is in place.

Request: T-P2

Peers: Logitech International (enterprise and gaming peripherals; raised US prices in 2025 to offset tariffs); HP Inc. and Poly (number two in enterprise headsets; history of deep discounting to win corporate contracts); Yealink Network Technology (low-cost certified entrant expanding in Europe); Cisco Systems (meeting-room video pricing); Corsair Gaming, Razer, Turtle Beach and HP's HyperX (gaming-peripheral pricing and promotions); EPOS under ACCO Brands (smaller enterprise headset rival); the distributors and resellers TD Synnex, Ingram Micro, CDW, Bechtle and Atea (channel pricing, marketing-fund demands and inventory policy). Operating conditions: distributor destocking under high interest rates (Europe, 2023 to 2026); US import tariffs and their pass-through to customers (2025); refunds or reversal of tariffs (2026); memory and semiconductor cost inflation (2026); a competitor's supply disruption (Logitech's semiconductor supplier problem, from July 2026); weak consumer demand in gaming; the launch phase and later phases of a product cycle (Jabra Evolve3, launched March 2026 with mid-tier models from September 2026); large corporate tenders in which PC makers bundle headsets with computers; entry of low-cost certified Chinese brands into European entry-level and mid-tier headsets. Inputs: (1) Which price increases GN applied in 2025 (about 10% in the US on Enterprise and Gaming, according to Jefferies on 26 August 2025 and DNB Carnegie on 1 May 2025), when they took effect, whether they were held through 2026, and the volume response by division and region. (2) After the US tariff refund of DKK 153 million recognised in Q3 2026 (Interim Report Q3 2026, 5 November 2026), whether GN and its peers have kept list prices, and which US tariffs remain on products made in Vietnam and Malaysia. (3) How much of GN's 2026 gross-margin improvement came from price, mix and cost respectively, including the Enterprise underlying gross margin of 57.2% in Q2 2026 (Jefferies, 20 August 2026) and the Gaming gross margin of 39.2% in Q2 2026, which included provision releases. (4) Whether retail discounting of Jabra products reflects distributors adjusting their own margins rather than GN's prices, as a CDW strategic account executive stated (expert call, 18 September 2024), and what marketing development funds large distributors demand. (5) How price-elastic gaming-peripheral demand is, and how SteelSeries' prices held against Logitech, Razer and Corsair promotions in 2025-2026. (6) Whether Yealink's and other low-cost brands' European pricing is pulling down entry-level and mid-tier enterprise headset prices, and whether corporate buyers are trading down. (7) In bundled PC-and-peripheral tenders by HP, Dell, Lenovo and Logitech, what discounts are given on headsets and how often Jabra wins. (8) What switching costs Microsoft Teams, Zoom and Google Workspace certification and Jabra+ device management create for corporate buyers. (9) How enterprise headset prices behaved during the 2023-2025 volume decline. (10) What GN's management, its competitors and the sell-side say now about pricing power in Enterprise and in Gaming. Focus: Whether GN's 2025 price increases will hold now that part of the tariff cost behind them has been refunded, turning a cost pass-through into a real price gain, and how much price can add to revenue and margin in Enterprise and in Gaming through a European downturn and more aggressive bundling.

Request: T-P3

Why requested: GN's results over 2027-2029 depend on several linked dynamics (buybacks and the share count, operating leverage on recovering volume, channel inventory, the installed-base refresh, and the value of the Amplifon shares that fund later distributions) that can amplify or offset one another. Peers: Logitech International (margin recovery, buybacks and net cash); HP Inc. and Poly (bundling against specialist vendors); Plantronics before its sale to HP (leverage followed by share loss); Amplifon (its share price sets the value of GN's 56 million Amplifon shares; integration of GN Hearing); Demant and Sonova (vertical integration in hearing care that affects Amplifon and independent audiologists); Corsair Gaming (product-cycle dynamics in gaming peripherals). Inputs: (1) The installed-base refresh: whether replacing headsets bought in 2020-2021 widens Jabra+ device-management fleets and certification lock-in, raising Jabra's share of the next refresh. (2) Channel inventory: whether, once distributor destocking ends, sell-in recovers only to the level of sell-out, and whether higher interest rates keep distributor stocks permanently lower. (3) Operating leverage and reinvestment: whether recovering margins fund R&D and a faster launch cadence that defends premium share, or whether cost cutting and lower capitalised development weaken the product pipeline. (4) Capital return: how share buybacks of the size GN plans (at least DKK 4 billion from closing to end-2027, company, 5 November 2026) affect EPS, the share price and value per share at different buyback prices, using precedents. (5) Amplifon: whether independent audiologists moving orders away from ReSound under Amplifon ownership lowers Amplifon's share price and with it the value of GN's Amplifon shares and GN's capacity for later distributions. (6) Bundling: whether PC makers' bundled offers progressively take share from specialist headset vendors in large corporate tenders. (7) Price and volume: whether the 2025 price increases suppress volume, especially in gaming. (8) Leverage: whether re-levering towards the 1.0x-1.5x gearing target (company, 5 November 2026) to fund distributions exposes the company to a downturn, as debt did after the SteelSeries acquisition in 2022. (9) Talent: whether staff departures during the carve-out slow product cadence and weaken share. Focus: Which of these dynamics will be strongest over 2027-2029, and whether the reinforcing ones (buybacks, installed-base refresh, operating leverage) outweigh the balancing ones (channel inventory, bundling, price elasticity, Amplifon's share price, re-levering).

Request: T-P4

Why requested: after the Hearing sale, the balance sheet, the planned buybacks, the tax on the transaction and the amortisation of acquired intangibles all change at once, so the items below operating profit and the share count are central inputs to GN's earnings per share for 2027 and 2028. Peers: Logitech International (EPS bridge with buybacks and net cash); Amplifon (value, dividends and liquidity of the shares GN holds); Demant (Danish tax and buyback-resumption precedent); Sonova (buyback and dividend policy in a cash-generative hearing peer); Corsair Gaming (amortisation of acquired intangibles in a gaming-peripheral business). Inputs: (1) The amortisation of acquired intangibles from the SteelSeries acquisition charged to continuing operations in 2026-2029, and the difference between adjusted EBITA and EBIT on brokers' bases. (2) Capitalised development spending against amortisation of capitalised development after the DKK 1.3 billion of non-cash impairments in 2026 (company, 6 May 2026), and the capitalisation policy going forward. (3) Net financial items after closing: repayment of gross debt (facilities maturing in 2028 and 2030), interest earned on cash, and dividends on the Amplifon shares. (4) Tax: the effective rate (22.4% in 2025, FactSet), the size of the tax asset created by the upfront tax payment now estimated at DKK 300-500 million (Q3 2026 earnings call, 5 November 2026), and how long it shields cash tax. (5) The share count: timing and prices of the Safe Harbour buyback of up to DKK 1 billion running to March 2027 and of the further DKK 3 billion to be authorised at the 2027 annual general meeting (Q3 2026 presentation, 5 November 2026), cancellation of treasury shares, and dilution from incentive programmes. (6) The opening net cash position after closing, with and without the Amplifon shares and lease liabilities (the company and DNB Carnegie cite net cash of DKK 5-6 billion, 5 and 9 November 2026), and the one-off carve-out cash costs remaining in 2027. (7) The Amplifon stake: current value, the lock-up schedule of 9 to 15 months after closing, and stated sell-down intentions. (8) The 19% stake in Nations Benefits, the LXE Hearing stake and Jabra Enhance: values and plans for sale. (9) The dividend under the reinstated payout policy of 20-40% of net profit (company, 5 November 2026). (10) What brokers assume for each of these items and how far apart their assumptions are. Focus: What share count, net financial result, tax charge and amortisation below EBITA GN will carry in 2027 and 2028, since these items, more than operating profit, explain the spread of brokers' EPS forecasts.

Layer 2: Translating the foundational components into an operating and financial model for the next 18 months, and 18 months to 3 years out

Written 24 November 2026, 08:07 to 08:21 UK, Opus, main login

What the Investment Analyst was asked

The objective is to produce a well-reasoned, coherent, deeply understood and accurate operating and financial model for the company for the next 18 months to four years, based on the first set's answers, a base of the company's historical financial and operating performance, and how businesses in the sector and industry outperform or underperform, so as to best help the Consumer achieve their objective, which is to produce a trustworthy judgement regarding the predictability of delivery of the financial forecasts over the next 18 months.

What the scores mean in this layer

Finish each answer and each group judgement with a score from 1 to 100; a higher number is more positive for an investment case in the company.

For this set of questions the score says how positive the likely effect on the company's financial forecasts is for an investment case in the company: 100 the most positive, 1 the most negative, 50 neither.

The Investment Analyst's title
GN Store Nord (GN-DK): answers to the second set of questions (financial forecasts), as at 24 November 2026

The Investment Analyst's opening notes

All figures are for "RemainCo" (Enterprise and Gaming; Hearing is discontinued and assumed to close in December 2026), in DKK millions unless stated. "Adjusted EBITA" is the company's definition: before amortisation of acquired intangibles (the SteelSeries purchase accounting, "PPA amortisation" below) and before special items. Every forecast figure is my estimate unless a source is named. The same numbers are entered in forecast-inputs.md.

The single most important reading of this table. Between 2027 and 2030, my EPS (model basis) compounds at about 26% a year, adjusted EBITA at about 10% a year and net income at only about 4% a year. The difference is the share count, which falls from 145.6m to about 68m at end-2030 as roughly DKK 10bn is returned on a market value of about DKK 16.7bn. From 2026 to 2027, the largest single EPS driver is not operations but the disappearance of interest cost. The Consumer should therefore judge predictability on three separate bridges with very different reliability: the per-share bridge (share count, interest), which is high-confidence and largely contracted; the cost bridge (DKK 200m programme), which is medium-high; and the volume bridge (European enterprise demand, gaming), which is low-confidence and is where almost all of the forecast risk sits.

The opening balance sheet is now reconciled (this resolves the first set's open item 6). The AlphaSense EPS-bridge report (24 November 2026) attributes the company's and DNB Carnegie's "net cash of DKK 5-6bn" to a figure that includes the Amplifon shares; DNB Carnegie's figure excluding them is about DKK 2bn (DKK 12.6bn cash less about DKK 9.5bn of debt, DKK 0.3-0.5bn of tax and about DKK 0.3bn of remaining Q4 carve-out costs). My end-2026 figure of about DKK 3.0bn net cash, excluding leases and the Amplifon stake, adds what DNB's crude bridge leaves out: Q4 2026 RemainCo free cash flow, including reversal of the DKK 205m inventory build, and Hearing's lease liabilities leaving with the business. The range I would hold is DKK 2.5-3.5bn, with the Amplifon stake on top at roughly DKK 3.0-4.4bn depending on Amplifon's share price (I use DKK 3.45bn as the realisable value).

Part 4. The reasoning behind the inputs

Perimeter and basis for every input. All inputs are for continuing operations only ("RemainCo": Enterprise and Gaming). Hearing is discontinued and is assumed to close in December 2026 (all approvals met 6 November 2026). Revenue growth is reported growth against the restated continuing FY2025 base of DKK 9,568m. EBITDA and its margin are on an adjusted basis: adjusted EBITA (company definition, before amortisation of acquired intangibles and special items) plus all depreciation and amortisation except the amortisation of acquired intangibles (that is, including amortisation of capitalised development and depreciation of right-of-use assets). Special items (carve-out costs, the 2026 impairments, the DKK -182m LXE write-off) are excluded from the P&L inputs; their cash is placed in "Acquisitions less disposals". FY2026 includes the DKK 153m US tariff refund booked in Q3 2026 (without it the FY2026 EBITDA margin is about 13.0%).

Segments: revenue and margin by segment, where the company reports them

The company reports two divisions, with "divisional profit" before shared costs (R&D, administration and central functions), which then sit as one block of about DKK 1.8-1.9bn. Divisional figures beyond FY2025 are my estimates; the allocation of shared costs changed during 2026 (the Q3 G&A correction), so the split is approximate.

Enterprise divisional margin 33.5% about 35% (incl. DKK 78m refund) about 35.5% about 36%

The operating drivers behind the inputs

  • Volume and organic growth. Q4 2026 about +5% organic (Gaming about +12% on easy comparisons and new releases; Enterprise about +2% after a FalCom deferral drag of about 2.5 points); currency roughly neutral from Q3 2026. 2027 about +3.5% organic and +3.0% reported (about -0.5 point of currency), front-loaded on easy H1 comparisons (Q1 2026 -4%, Q2 2026 -4%) and the full-year effect of Evolve3 mid-tier models (from September 2026); slowing to about +0.5% reported in Q4 2027 against the Q4 2026 gaming spike. 2028-2030 about +3.5% a year: market growth of 3-5% (DNB Carnegie), flat premium share, a slow European entry-level share loss to bundlers and low-cost certified entrants, and no structural gain in video or gaming. Revenue is deliberately below consensus (2027 DKK 9,730m against DKK 9,974m) because of GN's record of missed revenue guidance in every year since 2022.
  • Price and mix. Like-for-like price about +0.7% a year for the group (Enterprise about +1%, Gaming about 0%), plus about +0.5% from premium mix. The 2025 US price rise of about 10% has been lapped; its benefit, together with tariff relief, is already in the 2026 underlying gross margin (about 51.7-51.9% against 49.6% in FY2025). Price and mix roughly offset wage, memory and residual tariff inflation, so the gross margin is held at about 52%.
  • Operating leverage. About DKK 1.9bn of shared costs are largely fixed. Contribution on extra revenue is about 40-45%, but realised drop-through in recovery is set at about 30-40% after reinvestment and inflation; in a downturn it would be about 50-65%.
  • Cost programme. DKK 200m run-rate savings (about 300 roles, Warsaw finance centre): about DKK 30m in Q4 2026 and the full amount from Q1 2027. About DKK 120-130m reaches adjusted EBITA in 2027 after reinvestment. A further net DKK 100m or so is phased over 2028-2030. No R&D cuts.
  • Quarterly margin shape. FY margins are spread using the 2025 seasonal pattern (Q1 about 1.9 points below the year, Q2 about 0.5 below, Q3 and Q4 about 1.1-1.2 above). Q4 2026 adjusted EBITA DKK 340m (11.7%); 2027 quarters DKK 168m / 204m / 251m / 316m; Q1 2028 DKK 197m. Quarterly D&A (excluding acquired-intangible amortisation) about DKK 135m in Q4 2026, DKK 140m a quarter in 2027 and DKK 145m in Q1 2028.
  • D&A. Development amortisation rose to DKK 100m in Q3 2026 from DKK 66m as Evolve3 went into amortisation; Jefferies expects about 1 point more D&A from completing Evolve3. The 2026 impairments do not produce a visible amortisation tailwind, so D&A rises gently.
  • Capital spending. Capitalised development about DKK 600-650m a year (Q3 2026: DKK 150m capitalised of DKK 207m incurred) plus about DKK 100-130m of fixed assets and software. I reject the consensus fall in capital spending to DKK 502m by 2029, which would require R&D cuts or a switch to expensing.
  • Working capital. About 36% of last-twelve-month revenue at Q3 2026, inflated by a DKK 205m inventory build for Q4; about 32% at end-2026 and normalising slowly to 30% by 2030. A single distributor (DKK 2,001m of 2025 sales) limits how far terms can be pushed.
  • Lease payments. Not disclosed for RemainCo in the files; estimated at about DKK 110m a year (offices, warehouses, distribution hubs). Treat as an estimate to be checked against the FY2026 annual report.
  • Net financial items. FY2026 DKK -540m is before the DKK -182m LXE write-off (reported about DKK -720m). 2027 +DKK 50m: interest on average net cash of about DKK 1.4bn (+30), a dividend on the 56m Amplifon shares of about DKK 120m (+120), lease interest (-20), fees, hedging and negative carry on any debt not repaid at once (-80). Then negative as GN re-levers (-15 / -80 / -130). This assumes gross debt is repaid after closing; Bernstein's DKK -461m for 2027 does not, and I reject it.
  • Tax. 23% book rate (22.4% in 2025; Demant about 23%). Cash tax will be about DKK 150-250m a year lower in 2027-2028 thanks to the tax asset created by the DKK 300-500m upfront tax on the sale. That is not captured by the rate input and is upside to free cash flow.
  • Acquisitions less disposals. FY2026 +12,700 = DKK 12,600m cash consideration, less about DKK 400m of tax on the gain, less about DKK 560m of carve-out cash costs paid in 2026, plus about DKK 550m of Hearing lease liabilities leaving with the business and about DKK 500m of Hearing's own 2026 cash generation retained before closing (the last two are my estimates; together they let the model's end-2026 net cash land near my DKK 3.0bn excluding leases). FY2027 +960 = about one third of the Amplifon stake sold after the first lock-up release (about September 2027), about DKK 1,150m, less about DKK 190m of remaining carve-out cash costs. FY2028 +2,300 = the remaining two thirds of the Amplifon stake (lock-up ends about March 2028). I value the 56m Amplifon shares at about DKK 3.45bn realisable (DKK 4.4bn at announcement; Amplifon has since fallen). No sale of the 19% Nations Benefits stake (DNB Carnegie's guess DKK 1.5bn) is assumed; that is upside.
  • Buybacks. FY2026 about DKK 150m in December after closing. FY2027 DKK 3,850m: the remainder of the DKK 1bn Safe Harbour programme to about March 2027, then DKK 3bn after the AGM authorisation. FY2028 DKK 3,000m (Amplifon proceeds returned, as management has said it will), FY2029 DKK 2,000m and FY2030 DKK 1,500m, as GN moves towards its 1.0-1.5x net debt to EBITDA target (about 0.8x at end-2029 and about 1.3x at end-2030 in my base case). Assumed average buyback prices: DKK 118 (2026), 120-125 (2027), 130 (2028), 145 (2029), 160 (2030). Average shares: 145.6m, 129.3m, 102.2m, 84.0m, 72.7m.
  • Dividend per share. Policy 20-40% of net profit (reinstated 5 November 2026). I use about 25% of reported net profit (after acquired-intangible amortisation and special items), because management prefers buybacks. FY2026 nil: continuing net profit is negative after special items, and the DKK 4bn programme is the 2026-2027 distribution.
  • Change in share count other than buybacks. Treasury shares used for incentive programmes; about 0.3m a year (2.65m units outstanding, 1.8% of issued shares; options forfeited in 2025). Cancellation of treasury shares does not change the outstanding count.

The bridge from the company's own reported figures to the basis consensus uses

  • Revenue. The same on all bases (reported revenue, continuing operations). FactSet consensus revenue (DKK 9,510m / 9,974m / 10,412m / 10,827m for 2026-2029) is on the continuing perimeter. Note that FactSet's "brokers' basis" FY2025 actual (DKK 16,782m) is the old group; consensus growth for 2026 against it (-43%) is a perimeter artefact.
  • EBITDA. My EBITDA = company adjusted EBITA + D&A excluding acquired-intangible amortisation. Brokers' EBITDA in FactSet is on mixed bases and is not comparable: for 2027, Oddo BHF's DKK 1,556m (15.2%) is close to my definition, while DNB Carnegie's DKK 932m (9.4%) is below its own adjusted EBITA of DKK 1,007m, so it must be after special items or after development amortisation. The FactSet median of DKK 1,205m (12.1%) is a mix of the two and should not be read as conservatism. FactSet's history EBITDA (FactSet operating) is the old group and is not a base for RemainCo.
  • From my EBITDA to EPS (FY2027, DKKm). EBITDA 1,500, less D&A excluding acquired-intangible amortisation (-560), gives adjusted EBITA 940 (company basis). Less acquired-intangible amortisation (-220) gives adjusted EBIT 720, which is the basis closest to FactSet consensus EBIT (DKK 699m). Less special items (about -190) gives reported EBIT of about 530. Net financial items +50; tax at 23%.
  • EPS before acquired-intangible amortisation and special items (the model's basis, from these inputs): 5.90.
  • EPS after acquired-intangible amortisation, before special items (closest to most brokers' "adjusted" EPS): 4.59.
  • EPS after both (reported, continuing): about 3.46.
  • FactSet median 3.90 (mixed bases and broker sets; the median net income of DKK 420m and median EPS of 3.90 imply about 108m shares, against my time-weighted average of 129.3m).
  • Acquired-intangible amortisation (memo, not a model input). About -236 (2026), -220 (2027), -200 (2028), -180 (2029), -160 (2030). Bernstein holds -240 flat. Adding it back is the difference between the model's EPS and the brokers'.
  • Free cash flow. FactSet's own free cash flow definition is operating cash flow less capital spending on fixed assets only (it excludes capitalised development and leases), so consensus free cash flow (DKK 1,060m / 484m / 603m / 849m) is not comparable with mine, which deducts all capital spending, lease payments, interest and book tax and excludes special items.
  • Net debt. FactSet total debt includes lease liabilities (FactSet quirk 20). The company's net interest-bearing debt (DKK 9,578m at Q3 2026, 3.9x) also includes leases. My net cash figures exclude leases (RemainCo lease liabilities of about DKK 0.45-0.5bn are my estimate) and exclude the Amplifon stake.

Earnings per share is on the model's basis: before amortisation of acquired intangibles and before special items, on time-weighted average shares (145.6m, 129.3m, 102.2m, 84.0m, 72.7m).
