Layer 3 and Layer 4: Assessing the predictability of the financial forecasts for the next 18 months / Assessing the extent to which our assessment of the foundational components and financial forecasts is reflected or not in current company guidance, sell-side estimates and stock market narrative, primarily for the next 18 months and secondarily for 18 months to 3 years out

Written 25 November 2026, 05:50 to 06:19 UK, Fable, main login

What the Investment Analyst was asked

The objective is to produce a robustly tested assessment of the predictability of the financial forecasts for the next 18 months, based on the first and second sets' answers.

The objective is to produce that judgement, based on the first and second sets' answers and your answers to the third set, for the Investment Analyst who writes the investment memo, so that Richard, who reads the memo, can form his judgement on the investment case.

What the scores mean in this layer

Finish each answer and each judgement on a group or set with a score from 1 to 100; a higher number is more positive for an investment case in the company.

For the third set's questions, the score says how positive the thing the question asks about is for the predictability of the company's financial forecasts over the next 18 months, and so for an investment case in the company: 100 the most positive, 1 the most negative, 50 neither.

For the fourth set's questions, the score says how favourable for an investment case in the company the gap is between our assessment and what current guidance, sell-side estimates and the stock market narrative already reflect: 100 the most favourable, 1 the least, 50 neither.

The Investment Analyst's title
GN Store Nord (GN-DK): answers to the third and fourth sets of questions, as at 25 November 2026

The Investment Analyst's opening notes

Orientation for the Consumer: how to read the model, what the new research corrected, and my base case

Terms used throughout. "RemainCo" is the continuing company after the Hearing sale: Enterprise (Jabra, FalCom) and Gaming (SteelSeries). "PPA amortisation" is the amortisation of the intangibles acquired with SteelSeries, which sits below adjusted EBITA. "Model basis" earnings per share is before PPA amortisation and before special items, as the Financial Analyst's model calculates it. Money is in DKK millions unless stated. Forecast figures are my estimates unless a source is named.

  1. The gaps between the second set's cross-check and the model: what causes them

Judgement: the model's forecasts of record are overstated by about 40-55% on earnings per share from FY2027 (87% in FY2026) and by about 50% on free cash flow (far more in FY2026). Neither gap is a difference of view. About four fifths comes from one data fault in the model's base year. The rest comes from how the model counts shares. I have corrected the first through the inputs and asked for the second to be corrected in the model.

  • Cause 1: the revenue base contains a quarter of the old group.
  • FactSet holds the first three quarters of 2025 restated for continuing operations (DKK 2,283m, 2,302m, 2,211m). It holds Q4 2025 at DKK 4,678m, which still includes Hearing. The continuing figure is DKK 2,772m.
  • The model's 2025 base is therefore DKK 11,474m, not DKK 9,568m. Two checks confirm it. DKK 11,474m less 1.3% is exactly the model's FY2026 revenue of DKK 11,324.8m. And the model's note that it moved the Q4 2026 growth input by -2.6 points is what is needed to fit a +5.0% input to that base (Q4 2026 becomes DKK 4,791m, +2.4% on DKK 4,678m).
  • Revenue is 19.9% too high in every forecast year (FY2027: DKK 11,665m against the intended DKK 9,730m).
  • The margin input is applied to the inflated revenue, so EBITDA is also 19.9% too high (FY2027: DKK 1,796m against DKK 1,500m). Depreciation, amortisation and net financial items are entered as fixed amounts, so the whole excess falls through. FY2027 pre-tax profit is DKK 1,286m against DKK 990m, 30% too high.
  • Cause 2: the share count.
  • The model's earnings per share imply about 121m shares in 2027, 94m in 2028, 76m in 2029 and 62m in 2030. The second set used time-weighted averages of 129.3m, 102.2m, 84.0m and 72.7m.
  • The model's figures are reproduced almost exactly by buying back at a constant price of about DKK 120 and dividing each quarter's profit by the share count at the end of that quarter. That gives most weight to the seasonally large fourth quarter, when the count is lowest. This is my inference from the arithmetic, not a documented rule.
  • The second set assumed buyback prices rising from DKK 120-125 (2027) to DKK 160 (2030) and time-weighted averages. That is the more prudent treatment and the one I keep.
FY2026 FY2027 FY2028 FY2029 FY2030
Earnings per share: second set / model 1.74 / 3.25 5.90 / 8.21 7.85 / 11.24 9.87 / 14.49 11.76 / 18.35
Overstatement of net income from the revenue base +83% +30% +32% +32% +34%
Further overstatement from the share count +2% +7% +9% +11% +17%
Free cash flow: second set / model -379 / 1,682 454 / 675 495 / 739 522 / 778 538 / 814
  • Free cash flow, FY2027 to FY2030. The gap is wholly the revenue base. In FY2027 the model's EBITDA is DKK 296m higher, its tax DKK 68m higher and its working-capital build DKK 7m higher: DKK 221m in total.
  • Free cash flow, FY2026. Of the DKK 2,061m gap, DKK 274m is the overstated EBITDA, less DKK 63m of extra tax. The remaining DKK 1,849m is working capital. The model opens with the old group's balance of DKK 5,201m at end-2025 and closes at 32% of revenue, so it books a "release" of DKK 1,577m. That is Hearing's working capital leaving with the business, which the sale price already pays for. It is not cash generated. The second set assumed a build of DKK 272m.

What I have changed, and what the Financial Analyst should do.

  • I have rewritten the two revenue-growth inputs that touch the faulty quarter: FY2026 (-17.7%) and the quarter to 31 December 2026 (-37.8%). Applied to the model's existing base, they give the intended DKK 9,443m for FY2026 and DKK 2,910m for Q4 2026. They express the same view as the second set's -1.3% and +5.0% against the true continuing base.
  • After the update the model should show FY2027 revenue of about DKK 9,680m, EBITA of about DKK 911m, net income of about DKK 740m and net cash at end-2026 of about DKK 2.4bn. If it does not, the base has been changed as well.
  • The Financial Analyst should apply those two inputs to the base as it now stands and should not also restate Q4 2025. If the base is ever restated to the continuing DKK 2,772m for Q4 2025 (DKK 9,568m for the year), the two inputs must revert to +5.0% and -1.3%.
  • The Financial Analyst should set the opening working capital for FY2026 to the continuing balance, or otherwise remove the perimeter effect. Until then the model's FY2026 free cash flow (about DKK 2.1bn after the revenue correction) is an artefact. My figure is about DKK -315m.
  • The Financial Analyst should calculate earnings per share on time-weighted average shares, state the buyback price the model assumes, and show earnings per share after PPA amortisation beside the model basis. Until then the model's earnings per share will stay about 7% (2027) to 17% (2030) above mine for this reason alone.

  1. Two model outputs the memo must not use until this is corrected

  • The comparison with consensus. The model shows Viewforth 17-19% above consensus on revenue and 110% above on 2027 earnings per share. On the corrected figures we are about 3% below consensus on revenue and, like for like, close to it on 2027 earnings per share (I-02). The model's EBITDA also uses a wider definition than consensus (item 2 of section 3 below), so that comparison is invalid even after the base is corrected.
  • The return at the guideline fair multiple (+214.6% to 31 March 2028; DKK 376 a share). Three faults are stacked in it. 1. The earnings are overstated as above. 2. "12.3x today" is calculated on those earnings. On corrected figures the shares trade on about 17x two-year-forward earnings on the model basis, and about 22x after PPA amortisation, which is what FactSet shows (22.1x). 3. The guideline multiple of 24.79x is drawn from the ten-year history of a group that earned most of its profit from hearing aids. RemainCo is a cyclical hardware business whose earnings per share are being raised by buybacks. Its own five-year median was 11x.
  • Even after the base is corrected the model will still show a return of about +100% at 24.79x. That would be a statement about the multiple and nothing else. On the first set's 8-11x EBITA, or about 14-16x earnings after PPA amortisation (my judgement), the value at the 18-month point is roughly DKK 95-135 a share. That is around today's DKK 115-120, not three times it.

  1. What the new research corrected in the first two sets

  1. Consensus operating profit carries no credibility discount. - The company's and the brokers' "EBITDA" adds back only about DKK 45m a quarter (Q3 2026: EBITDA DKK 457m against adjusted EBITA DKK 413m). Amortisation of capitalised development is not added back. This is my reading of those two figures and of consensus depreciation of about DKK 200m a year, with about 75% confidence; my first research request asks for the definition to be confirmed. - Consensus 2027 EBITDA of DKK 1,205m less consensus depreciation of DKK 208m is therefore an adjusted EBITA of about DKK 1.0bn, a 10.0% margin. That matches DNB Carnegie (DKK 1,007m) and the DKK 1,010m the guidance research report shows for Bernstein (the second set had DKK 907m for Bernstein). - The first set read consensus EBIT of 7.0% as sitting below the CFO's 10-11%, "part definition and part credibility" (C-68). It is all definition: EBIT of DKK 699m plus PPA amortisation of about DKK 240m plus about DKK 60m of special items. Consensus is at the bottom of the CFO's range, and above us.
  2. Three definitions of EBITDA are in play and must not be mixed. The model's (adjusted EBITA plus all depreciation and amortisation except PPA: 15.2% in 2027 on my inputs). The company's and the brokers' (about 2 points above EBITA: about 11.4% on my inputs against consensus of 12.1%). And FactSet's history, which adds back everything.
  3. The expected mid-term margin target is ambiguous. One research report cites DNB Carnegie (9 November 2026) as expecting a target of about 12% EBITA. Another cites the same note as expecting about 12% EBITDA, with guidance moving to EBITDA from 2027 under the new accounting standard IFRS 18. On the company's definition a 12% EBITDA margin is an EBITA margin of about 10%. Both earlier sets assumed EBITA. This changes how high the bar is (I-01).
  4. Development costs: the second set's figure was wrong. - In Q3 2026 GN capitalised DKK 150m of DKK 257m incurred, 58.4%. The second set had DKK 150m of DKK 207m (72%). Over nine months the rate was 52.3% (DKK 417m of DKK 797m), lower than a year earlier and inside the historical 50-57%. Capitalisation is not being stretched (P-03). - Incurred development cost is about DKK 1.06bn a year, 11% of revenue, not DKK 830m. - Amortisation has risen each quarter of 2026 (DKK 91m, 98m, 100m). That confirms the second set against the first: the impairments gave no relief.
  5. Leases are much smaller. Continuing lease liabilities were DKK 134m at Q3 2026 (DKK 35m current). Hearing's were DKK 325m. The second set assumed DKK 450-500m and DKK 550m. Lease payments are about DKK 40-45m a year, not DKK 110m, which adds about DKK 65m a year to free cash flow.
  6. The Amplifon stake is worth more than the second set allowed. - At about EUR 11.2 a share (2 November 2026, after the 15% fall on Amplifon's Q3 results) the 56m shares are worth about DKK 4.7bn. The second set used DKK 3.45bn "realisable", a 26% discount to market. - I use DKK 4.0bn: market less about 6% for placement discounts (the one precedent cited was 5%) and a further allowance for an overhang equal to 35% of Amplifon's free float. - Non-operating value (net cash of about DKK 2.5bn after closing, Amplifon, Nations Benefits) is then about DKK 50-55 a share, about 45% of the share price.
  7. Debt can be repaid cheaply. It is a floating-rate EUR 937m term loan, short-term lines and commercial paper, plus about EUR 95m of private placements to 2036 at 2.0-3.2% that are worth keeping. The second set's +DKK 50m of net financial items in 2027 stands. Its swing item is the Amplifon dividend (about DKK 120m at EUR 0.29 a share, paid in May), which is not assured at Amplifon's leverage of 3.3x. A one-off write-off of capitalised loan fees is likely at repayment (DKK 50m in a 2025 precedent).
  8. How the Amplifon stake will be accounted for is undecided, and no broker models it. A board seat and a shareholders' agreement point to equity accounting, under which GN would book about 17% of Amplifon's profit and no dividend income. Otherwise fair-value changes of about DKK 470m for each 10% move in Amplifon's price go through the income statement or through equity. Reported 2027 earnings will carry this noise.
  9. Q3 2026 was weaker underneath than the first set showed. Excluding both the tariff refund and the administrative-cost correction, the margin was 8.8% (DNB Carnegie), the same as Q3 2025, despite 2% organic growth. Enterprise's underlying gross margin was flat at 55.8%.
  10. The cost of standing alone is not settled. Transitional service agreements with Amplifon run for up to 24 months and their fees are undisclosed. DNB Carnegie notes a DKK 416m difference between GN's and Amplifon's statements of Hearing's EBITDA, which it attributes to unallocated group overhead. GN puts stranded costs at DKK 200m.
  11. The gearing target is on the company's EBITDA. The reported 3.9x fits that definition (net debt of DKK 9.6bn over EBITDA of about DKK 2.4-2.5bn for the whole group); this is my inference. On it, the second set's net debt of DKK 2.5bn in 2030 is about 1.7x, above the 1.0-1.5x target, not the 1.3x it stated. With my higher Amplifon value and lower leases the same buybacks give about 1.6x. The DKK 6.5bn of buybacks in the inputs for 2028-2030 is therefore the top of a fair range of DKK 5.5-6.5bn.
  12. Grand Theft Auto 6 was released on 19 November 2026 (consoles only; about 19m copies on the first day, per Morgan Stanley). The first set called it upcoming.

Co-movement of GN-DK with its dashboard peers (computed 2026-11-24 from the master dashboard's daily prices)

What this is: daily close-to-close returns over rolling windows of 126 trading days (about six months). Correlation: how closely the daily moves line up (1 = always together, 0 = unrelated). Beta: how far the stock moves for a 1% move in the basket. R-squared: the share of the stock's daily price variance the peer basket explains; the remainder is the stock's own (idiosyncratic) movement. Basket: the peers below, equal-weighted. Prices only; the multiple's own history is in the financials block.

Appendix Research requests the Investment Analyst raised for the memo

Research requests for the next set

Company: GN Store Nord A/S (GN-DK), a Danish maker of audio and gaming hardware which, after selling its Hearing division to Amplifon (agreed 16 March 2026, closing expected by end-2026), consists of the Enterprise division (Jabra office headsets, video collaboration devices, BlueParrott and FalCom tactical communication headsets) and the Gaming division (SteelSeries gaming headsets, keyboards, mice and software)

Request: OWN-1

Question: For GN Store Nord (GN-DK), which profit measure will the company use for its FY2027 guidance at its results on 11 February 2027 and for the mid-term targets at its Capital Markets Day on 24 February 2027, exactly how is that measure defined, and what do brokers expect the guidance and targets to be? Specifically: (a) how does GN Store Nord define EBITDA and EBITA in its annual and interim reports, and is amortisation of capitalised development projects added back in its EBITDA or left inside it (the Q3 2026 presentation of 5 November 2026 shows continuing-operations EBITDA of DKK 457 million under IFRS 5 and DKK 459 million under IFRS 18, against adjusted EBITA of DKK 413 million for the quarter); (b) what the adoption of IFRS 18 changes in GN's income statement, its alternative performance measures and its guidance from 2027; (c) on which EBITDA definition the company's leverage target of 1.0-1.5 times net debt to EBITDA and its reported 3.9 times at Q3 2026 are calculated; (d) what each covering broker (DNB Carnegie, Bernstein, Jefferies, Jyske Bank, Oddo BHF, Sydbank, Barclays, Morgan Stanley, AlphaValue) expects for the FY2027 guidance ranges and for the Capital Markets Day targets, stating for each expectation whether the margin is an EBITA margin or an EBITDA margin and on which definition (DNB Carnegie's note of 9 November 2026 is cited as expecting organic growth of 3-6% a year and a margin target of about 12% for 2027-2030: is that 12% an EBITA margin or an EBITDA margin?), and what each expects the company to say about capital allocation after 2027, including the use of proceeds from the 56 million Amplifon shares and any second distribution programme; (e) how GN's initial annual guidance ranges for 2022 to 2026 compared with consensus on the day they were given, how the share price reacted on the day and over the following month, and at which quarterly report each range was later changed; and (f) what happened at comparable resets of targets and at first stand-alone guidance after a divestment or carve-out (for example Demant's Capital Markets Day of 23 November 2026, Logitech's analyst day of March 2025, Sonova's strategy update of March 2026, Corsair Gaming, Turtle Beach and other hardware or medical-technology companies): where the targets were set against consensus, how estimates and share prices moved in the following weeks, and how often the targets were later met, raised or abandoned.

Request: OWN-2

Question: For GN Store Nord (GN-DK), reconcile each covering broker's forecasts for FY2026, FY2027 and FY2028 line by line from revenue to earnings per share for the continuing Enterprise and Gaming business, and explain why the brokers' earnings-per-share forecasts are so far apart. For each of DNB Carnegie, Bernstein, Jefferies, Jyske Bank, Oddo BHF, Sydbank, Barclays, Morgan Stanley, AlphaValue and any other covering house, give, with the date of the note: revenue and organic growth by division; adjusted EBITA; EBITDA and the definition used; amortisation of acquired intangibles; special items and carve-out costs, and whether they are inside or outside the broker's adjusted earnings; net financial items split into interest expense, interest income and any dividend, associate income or fair-value change from the Amplifon shares; the tax rate; the average and the year-end share count; the size, timing and assumed price of share buybacks in 2026, 2027 and 2028; dividends per share; capital expenditure and capitalised development costs; free cash flow and its definition; and the definition of the earnings-per-share figure each broker submits to consensus. FactSet's median for FY2027 earnings per share was DKK 3.90 on 19 November 2026, with named brokers at DKK 4.00 (Jyske Bank), DKK 3.81 (DNB Carnegie) and DKK 4.86 (Oddo BHF) and Bernstein at about DKK 1.05-1.40, while the same brokers' adjusted EBITA forecasts for FY2027 are much closer together: which line items explain that dispersion; which brokers have and which have not updated their models for repayment of debt at closing, for the reduction of the upfront tax payment to DKK 300-500 million, for the DKK 1 billion Safe Harbour buyback running to 9 March 2027 and for the DKK 3 billion to be authorised at the annual general meeting on 10 March 2027; and how does each broker treat the 56 million Amplifon shares in the income statement and balance sheet (as an associate under IAS 28, at fair value through profit and loss, or at fair value through other comprehensive income)? Also give each broker's fourth-quarter 2026 forecasts for organic growth by division and for adjusted EBITA, state how each compares with the company's FY2026 guidance (organic growth of 0-3% and an adjusted EBITA margin of 9-10%), and list every change of estimate, rating and target price since 5 November 2026 with the reason the broker gave.

Request: OWN-3

Question: What multiple of earnings is appropriate for GN Store Nord's (GN-DK) continuing Enterprise and Gaming business, judged by how the stock market values comparable hardware companies at different points in their cycle, and how do brokers value GN Store Nord today? Give: (a) the current and the five-year and ten-year ranges of EV/EBITA, EV/EBITDA, EV/sales and forward price-to-earnings multiples for Logitech International, Corsair Gaming, Turtle Beach, HP Inc., Dell Technologies, Yealink Network Technology, ACCO Brands, Cisco Systems and Sonos, and for Plantronics (Poly) before its sale to HP in 2022, stating for each whether development costs are expensed or capitalised and whether amortisation of acquired intangibles is deducted; (b) the multiples these shares traded on at their earnings troughs, in the first year of recovery and at peak margins (for example Logitech in 2022-2024, and Corsair Gaming and Turtle Beach in 2022-2026), and what moved the multiple in each phase (volume growth, margin, buybacks, or the sector as a whole); (c) each covering broker's valuation of GN Store Nord since March 2026 (DNB Carnegie, Bernstein, Jefferies, Jyske Bank, Oddo BHF, Barclays, Morgan Stanley, AlphaValue): the method, the target multiple for the continuing business, the discount or premium to Logitech and the reason for it, the values placed on net cash after closing, on the 56 million Amplifon shares, on the 19% stake in Nations Benefits and on any other asset in a sum-of-the-parts, and the target price with any bull and bear cases; (d) how GN Store Nord's own forward price-to-earnings and EV/EBITA multiples should be read through the change of perimeter in 2026, given that FactSet showed a 24-month-forward price-to-earnings multiple of about 22 times on 13 November 2026 against a five-year median of about 11 times, and what its multiples were when it was valued mainly as a hearing-aid company; (e) precedents of companies that returned 20% or more of their market value within two years through buybacks funded by a divestment: how the share price and the multiple behaved before the buyback began, while it ran and after it ended, and whether the shares re-rated or de-rated once the cash had been spent; and (f) the co-movement of GN Store Nord's share price with Logitech's and with Amplifon's over the last two years, the short interest in GN Store Nord, changes among its largest shareholders since March 2026, and any statement by William Demant Invest or the William Demant Foundation about its holding of about 12.3%.
